Implement the following plan:

# Plan: Unified cargo runner + --verbose support

## Context

`build_package()` (cargo_build.rs, 134 lines) and `test_package()` (testing.rs, 126 lines) share ~80% of their code. The long-standing todo item "Refactor `build_package`, `test_package`, `plain_cargo_build_release` into a unified cargo runner" addresses this. Adding `--verbose` on top of the unified runner means verbose printing lives in one place instead of three.

## Workflow: Multi-commit on branch `feat-unified-runner`

Version: `0.13.0` → `0.14.0` (new feature).

- `Cargo.toml` — Bump to `0.14.0-dev1`
- `notes/chores-5.md` — Add `## 20260219 - Unified cargo runner + verbose` entry with context and plan
- `notes/todo.md` — Move refactor item to In Progress

Message: `chore: Start feat-unified-runner branch, v0.14.0-dev1`

### Commit 2: refactor — Unify build_package/test_package into run_cargo()

**New types in `src/cargo_build.rs`:**
```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum CargoMode {
    Build,
    Test,
}
```

`BuildResult` stays as-is (no rename needed — test callers just ignore the fields).

**New function `run_cargo()` in `src/cargo_build.rs`:**
```rust
pub fn run_cargo(
    mode: CargoMode,
    pkg_name: &str,
    tspec: Option<&str>,
    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
) -> Result<BuildResult>
```

Absorbs the shared pattern:
1. Path resolution (find_project_root, find_package_dir, find_tspec, get_package_name)
2. Stale build.rs cleanup
3. Spec loading + target_dir expansion
4. Profile validation
5. Misconfiguration warnings
6. Build.rs generation (linker.args + bin target)
7. Command construction (cargo + toolchain + subcommand + -p)
8. `apply_spec_to_command()`
9. Test-only: append `-Zpanic_abort_tests` to RUSTFLAGS (gated on `mode == Test`)
10. `cmd.status()` + cleanup + error handling
11. Build-only: print binary path (gated on `mode == Build`)

**Nightly detection unification** — merge `requires_nightly()` and `requires_nightly_for_test()`:
```rust
fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> bool {
    let panic_needs = match mode {
        CargoMode::Test => spec.panic.map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some()).unwrap_or(false),
        CargoMode::Build => spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false),
    };
    panic_needs || !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty() || !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty()
}
```

`build_cargo_command()` takes `mode` parameter.

Move `needs_panic_abort_tests()` from testing.rs into cargo_build.rs.

**Keep thin wrappers** for backward compatibility (callers unchanged):
```rust
pub fn build_package(name, tspec, profile) -> Result<BuildResult> { run_cargo(Build, ...) }
pub fn test_package(name, tspec, profile) -> Result<()> { run_cargo(Test, ...); Ok(()) }
```

**Delete `src/testing.rs`**, update imports:

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `src/lib.rs` | Remove `pub mod testing;` |
| `src/all.rs` | `use crate::testing::test_package` → `use crate::cargo_build::test_package` |
| `src/cmd/test.rs` | `use crate::testing::test_package` → `use crate::cargo_build::test_package` |

Message: `refactor: Unify build_package/test_package into run_cargo(), v0.14.0-dev2`

### Commit 3: feat — Add --verbose/-v/-vv support

**Verbosity enum in `src/types.rs`** (already imported everywhere, avoids circular deps):
```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub enum Verbosity {
    #[default]
    Normal,    // no flag
    Verbose,   // -v: print command line + env vars
    Debug,     // -vv: also print spec resolution details
}
```

**CLI (`src/cli.rs`):**
```rust
pub struct Cli {
    #[arg(short = 'v', long = "verbose", action = ArgAction::Count, global = true)]
    pub verbose: u8,
    #[command(subcommand)]
    pub command: Commands,
}
```

**Execute trait (`src/cmd/mod.rs`):**
```rust
pub trait Execute {
    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, verbosity: Verbosity) -> Result<ExitCode>;
}
```

**Threading:** `main.rs` extracts verbosity from Cli, passes to execute(). Each command impl threads it down:
- Build/Test/Run/Compare → `run_cargo()` / `build_package()` / `test_package()` / batch `_all()` functions
- Clean/Clippy/Fmt → `execute_cargo_subcommand()`
- Ts/Version/Install → accept param but don't use it (yet)

**Updated function signatures** (add `verbosity: Verbosity`):
- `run_cargo()`, `build_package()`, `test_package()`, `plain_cargo_build_release()`
- `execute_cargo_subcommand()`
- `build_all()`, `test_all()`, `run_all()`, `compare_all()` in all.rs
- `compare_specs()` in compare.rs

**Verbose printing helper in `src/cargo_build.rs`:**
```rust
fn print_verbose_command(cmd: &Command, verbosity: Verbosity) {
    if verbosity >= Verbosity::Verbose {
        // Print: [verbose] cargo +nightly build -p pkg --target ...
        // Print: [verbose] RUSTFLAGS=...
        // Print: [verbose] TSPEC_SPEC_FILE=...
    }
}
```

**-v output** (before `cmd.status()`):
```
[verbose] cargo +nightly test -p ex-x2 --target x86_64-unknown-linux-rlibcx2 -Z build-std=std,core
[verbose] RUSTFLAGS=-C panic=abort -C strip=symbols -Zpanic_abort_tests
[verbose] TSPEC_SPEC_FILE=/path/to/tspec-optS.ts.toml
```

**-vv output** (before command construction):
```
[debug] spec: apps/ex-x2/tspec-optS.ts.toml
[debug] toolchain: nightly (from spec)
[debug] target_dir: tspec-optS-c08d154d (expanded from "{name}-{hash}")
[debug] effective profile: release (from spec)
```

**Files changed:**
- `src/types.rs` — Verbosity enum
- `src/cli.rs` — verbose field on Cli
- `src/main.rs` — extract verbosity, pass to execute()
- `src/cmd/mod.rs` — Execute trait signature, execute_cargo_subcommand signature
- `src/cmd/build.rs` — thread verbosity
- `src/cmd/test.rs` — thread verbosity
- `src/cmd/run.rs` — thread verbosity
- `src/cmd/compare.rs` — thread verbosity
- `src/cmd/clean.rs` — thread verbosity

- `src/cmd/fmt.rs` — thread verbosity

- `src/compare.rs` — thread verbosity
- `README.md` — document --verbose

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};

    11→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};
    12→use crate::types::{Spec, flatten_config};
    13→
    14→const TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER: &str = "// Generated by tspec - do not edit";
    15→
    16→/// Built-in cargo profiles that don't require a `[profile.<name>]` definition.
    17→const BUILTIN_PROFILES: &[&str] = &["dev", "debug", "release", "test", "bench"];
    18→
    19→/// Validate that a custom profile is defined in the workspace Cargo.toml.
    20→/// Built-in profiles (dev, release, test, bench) are always valid.
    21→/// Custom profiles must have a `[profile.<name>]` section with `inherits`.
    22→pub fn validate_profile(profile: &str, workspace: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    23→    if BUILTIN_PROFILES.contains(&profile) {
    24→        return Ok(());
    25→    }
    26→    let cargo_toml_path = workspace.join("Cargo.toml");
    27→    let content = fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml_path)
    28→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml_path.display()))?;
    29→    let doc: toml::Value =
    30→        toml::from_str(&content).context("failed to parse workspace Cargo.toml")?;
    31→    if let Some(profiles) = doc.get("profile").and_then(|p| p.as_table())
    32→        && profiles.contains_key(profile)
    33→    {
    34→        return Ok(());
    35→    }
    36→    bail!(
    37→        "profile `{}` is not defined in {}. Custom profiles must be defined with \
    38→         `[profile.{}]` and an `inherits` field (e.g., `inherits = \"release\"`).",
    39→        profile,
    40→        cargo_toml_path.display(),
    41→        profile,
    42→    )
    43→}
    44→/// Check for spec settings that are likely misconfigurations.
    45→/// Returns a list of warning messages (printed at top and bottom of output).
    46→pub fn check_spec_misconfigurations(pkg_name: &str, spec: &Spec, pkg_dir: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
    47→    let mut warnings = Vec::new();
    48→    let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    49→    let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
    50→
    51→    // -static with non-musl target (glibc + -static segfaults)
    52→    if has_linker_args {
    53→        let has_static = spec.linker.args.iter().any(|a| a == "-static");
    54→        let is_musl = spec
    55→            .cargo
    56→            .target_triple
    57→            .as_ref()
    58→            .is_some_and(|t| t.contains("musl"));
    59→        if has_static && !is_musl {
    60→            warnings.push(format!(
    61→                "Warning: -static linker arg without musl target for {}. \
    62→                 glibc + -static often segfaults; consider using a musl target triple.",
    63→                pkg_name
    64→            ));
    65→        }
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    // linker.args on lib-only package
    69→    if has_linker_args && !has_bin_target {
    70→        warnings.push(format!(
    71→            "Warning: linker.args ignored for {} (no binary target)",
    72→            pkg_name
    73→        ));
    74→    }
    75→
    76→    warnings
    77→}
    78→
    79→/// Print collected warnings again so they're visible after cargo output.
    80→pub fn reprint_warnings(warnings: &[String]) {
    81→    for w in warnings {
    82→        println!("{}", w);
    83→    }
    84→}
    85→
    86→pub fn warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale: bool) {
    87→    if had_stale {
    88→        println!("Warning: a stale build.rs was deleted, likely from a previous interrupted build");
    89→    }
    90→}
    91→
    92→/// Check if a build.rs file was generated by tspec.
    93→/// Matches either the marker comment or a file that only contains
    94→/// `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` println lines (marker-less variant).
    95→fn is_tspec_generated_build_rs(content: &str) -> bool {
    96→    if content.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER) {
    97→        return true;
    98→    }
    99→    // Fallback: file only contains fn main, cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin printlns, and braces
   100→    let dominated_by_link_args = content
   101→        .lines()
   102→        .map(|l| l.trim())
   103→        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
   104→        .all(|l| {
   105→            l == "fn main() {"
   106→                || l == "}"
   107→                || l.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER)
   108→                || l.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   109→        });
   110→    dominated_by_link_args && content.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   111→}
   112→
   113→/// Remove a stale tspec-generated build.rs left behind by an interrupted build.
   114→/// Returns true if a stale file was removed. Caller should print a warning after the build.
   115→pub fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
   116→    let build_rs = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   117→    if let Ok(content) = fs::read_to_string(&build_rs)
   118→        && is_tspec_generated_build_rs(&content)
   119→    {
   120→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs);
   121→        return true;
   122→    }
   123→    false
   124→}
   125→
   126→/// Result of a successful build
   127→pub struct BuildResult {
   128→    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
   129→    /// The effective target directory base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
   130→    pub target_base: PathBuf,
   131→}
   132→
   133→/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.
   134→/// `cli_profile` is the profile from the CLI (e.g., "release", "release-small").
   135→/// `None` means debug (default). `Some("release")` is equivalent to `--release`.
   136→pub fn build_package(
   137→    pkg_name: &str,
   138→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   139→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   140→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   141→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   142→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
   143→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
   144→
   145→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
   146→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
   147→
   148→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
   149→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
   150→
   151→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
   152→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   153→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
   154→
   155→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
   156→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
   157→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
   158→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
   159→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
   160→        (Some(s), td)
   161→    } else {
   162→        (None, None)
   163→    };
   164→
   165→    // Compute paths
   166→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
   167→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   168→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   169→    };
   170→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   171→        get_binary_path(
   172→            &workspace,
   173→            &pkg_name,
   174→            spec,
   175→            cli_profile,
   176→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   177→        )
   178→    } else {
   179→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, cli_profile)
   180→    };
   181→
   182→    // Validate the effective profile exists before invoking cargo
   183→    let effective_profile = spec
   184→        .as_ref()
   185→        .and_then(|s| s.cargo.profile.as_deref())
   186→        .or(cli_profile);
   187→    if let Some(profile) = effective_profile {
   188→        validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;
   189→    }
   190→
   191→    // Check for misconfigurations before running cargo
   192→    let spec_warnings = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   193→        check_spec_misconfigurations(&pkg_name, spec, &pkg_dir)
   194→    } else {
   195→        Vec::new()
   196→    };
   197→
   198→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo build
   199→    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   200→        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
   201→        println!("Building {} with spec {}", pkg_name, path.display());
   202→
   203→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
   204→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
   205→        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
   206→        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {
   207→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;
   208→        }
   209→
   210→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec)?;
   211→        cmd.arg("build");
   212→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   213→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   214→
   215→        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs
   216→        cmd.env("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE", path.as_os_str());
   217→
   218→        apply_spec_to_command(
   219→            &mut cmd,
   220→            spec,
   221→            &workspace,
   222→            cli_profile,
   223→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   224→        )?;
   225→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
   226→    } else {
   227→        println!("Building {} (no tspec)", pkg_name);
   228→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   229→        cmd.arg("build");
   230→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   231→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   232→        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   233→            match p {
   234→                "debug" | "dev" => {}
   235→                _ => {
   236→                    cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   237→                }
   238→            }
   239→        }
   240→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
   241→    };
   242→
   243→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
   244→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
   245→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
   246→    }
   247→
   248→    if !status.success() {
   249→        match &tspec_path {
   250→            Some(path) => {
   251→                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();
   252→                bail!(
   253→                    "cargo build failed for `{}` with spec {}",
   254→                    pkg_name,
   255→                    display_path
   256→                )
   257→            }
   258→            None => bail!("cargo build failed for `{}`", pkg_name),
   259→        }
   260→    }
   261→
   262→    println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   263→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   264→    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);
   265→    Ok(BuildResult {
   266→        binary_path,
   267→        target_base,
   268→    })
   269→}
   270→
   271→/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.
   272→/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.
   273→pub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   274→    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some("release"))
   275→}
   276→
   277→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   278→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   279→    let mut lines = vec![TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER.to_string(), "fn main() {".to_string()];
   280→
   281→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   282→        lines.push(format!(
   283→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   284→            crate_name, arg
   285→        ));
   286→    }
   287→
   288→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   289→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   290→    Ok(())
   291→}
   292→
   293→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain
   294→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   295→    // High-level panic mode may require nightly
   296→    let panic_needs_nightly = spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false);
   297→
   298→    // BuildStd requires nightly
   299→    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();
   300→
   301→    // Unstable cargo flags require nightly
   302→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   303→
   304→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   305→}
   306→
   307→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   308→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec) -> Result<Command> {
   309→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   310→
   311→    if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   312→        cmd.arg(format!("+{}", tc));
   313→    } else if requires_nightly(spec) {
   314→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   315→    }
   316→
   317→    Ok(cmd)
   318→}
   319→
   320→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command.
   321→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   322→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   323→    cmd: &mut Command,
   324→    spec: &Spec,
   325→    workspace: &Path,
   326→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   327→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   328→) -> Result<()> {
   329→    // Set custom target directory if specified
   330→    if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
   331→        cmd.arg("--target-dir")
   332→            .arg(workspace.join("target").join(td));
   333→    }
   334→
   335→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   336→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   337→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   338→    {
   339→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   340→    }
   341→
   342→    // Handle cargo config
   343→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   344→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   345→        match profile.as_str() {
   346→            "debug" | "dev" => {
   347→                // Debug/dev is default, no flag needed
   348→            }
   349→            _ => {
   350→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(profile);
   351→            }
   352→        }
   353→    }
   354→
   355→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   356→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   357→    }
   358→
   359→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   360→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg("json-target-spec");
   361→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   362→    }
   363→
   364→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   365→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   366→    }
   367→
   368→    // Inject --config key=value pairs (flattened from potentially nested config)
   369→    for (key, value) in flatten_config(&spec.cargo.config) {
   370→        cmd.arg("--config").arg(format!("{}={}", key, value));
   371→    }
   372→
   373→    // If no profile in spec, fall back to CLI profile
   374→    if !has_profile && let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   375→        match p {
   376→            "debug" | "dev" => {} // default, no flag needed
   377→            _ => {
   378→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   379→            }
   380→        }
   381→    }
   382→
   383→    // Collect rustc flags
   384→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   385→
   386→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   387→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   388→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   389→    {
   390→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   391→    }
   392→
   393→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   394→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   395→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   396→    {
   397→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   398→    }
   399→
   400→    if !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty() {
   401→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   402→        let crates_str = spec.cargo.build_std.join(",");
   403→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   404→    }
   405→
   406→    for flag in &spec.rustflags {
   407→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   408→    }
   409→
   410→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   411→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   412→        let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
   413→            Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   414→            None => workspace.join("target"),
   415→        };
   416→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
   417→        let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   418→
   419→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   420→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   421→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   422→
   423→        let mut f =
   424→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   425→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   426→
   427→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   428→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   429→            version_script_path.display()
   430→        ));
   431→    }
   432→
   433→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   434→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   435→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   436→    }
   437→
   438→    Ok(())
   439→}

   557→    #[test]
   558→    fn config_emits_config_args() {
   559→        use crate::types::ConfigValue;
   560→        use std::collections::BTreeMap;
   561→
   562→        let mut spec = Spec::default();
   563→        spec.cargo.config = BTreeMap::from([
   564→            (
   565→                "profile.release.opt-level".to_string(),
   566→                ConfigValue::String("s".to_string()),
   567→            ),
   568→            ("profile.release.lto".to_string(), ConfigValue::Bool(true)),
   569→            (
   570→                "profile.release.codegen-units".to_string(),
   571→                ConfigValue::Integer(1),
   572→            ),
   573→        ]);
   574→
   575→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   576→        cmd.arg("build");
   577→        let workspace = PathBuf::from("/tmp/fake");
   578→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, &spec, &workspace, None, None).unwrap();
   579→
   580→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd.get_args().map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into()).collect();
   581→
   582→        // BTreeMap is sorted, so codegen-units < lto < opt-level
   583→        let config_positions: Vec<usize> = args
   584→            .iter()
   585→            .enumerate()
   586→            .filter(|(_, a)| *a == "--config")
   587→            .map(|(i, _)| i)
   588→            .collect();
   589→        assert_eq!(config_positions.len(), 3);
   590→
   591→        // Check each --config arg
   592→        assert_eq!(
   593→            args[config_positions[0] + 1],
   594→            "profile.release.codegen-units=1"
   595→        );
   596→        assert_eq!(args[config_positions[1] + 1], "profile.release.lto=true");
   597→        assert_eq!(
   598→            args[config_positions[2] + 1],
   599→            "profile.release.opt-level=\"s\""
   600→        );
   601→    }

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
     3→use std::process::Command;

     5→use crate::cargo_build::{
     6→    apply_spec_to_command, check_spec_misconfigurations, generate_build_rs,
     7→    remove_stale_tspec_build_rs, reprint_warnings, validate_profile, warn_stale_build_rs,
     8→};
     9→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_project_root, find_tspec, get_package_name};
    10→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};
    11→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain for testing.
    12→/// This is stricter than the build version: panic=abort also needs nightly
    13→/// because `-Zpanic_abort_tests` is a nightly-only flag.
    14→fn requires_nightly_for_test(spec: &crate::types::Spec) -> bool {
    15→    let panic_needs_nightly = spec
    16→        .panic
    17→        .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some()) // any non-unwind panic needs nightly for tests
    18→        .unwrap_or(false);
    19→
    20→    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();
    21→
    22→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
    23→
    24→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
    25→}
    26→
    27→/// Check if spec uses an abort-like panic mode that needs `-Zpanic_abort_tests` for testing.
    28→fn needs_panic_abort_tests(spec: &crate::types::Spec) -> bool {
    29→    spec.panic
    30→        .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some()) // abort or immediate-abort
    31→        .unwrap_or(false)
    32→}

    34→/// Test a package with a spec.
    35→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
    36→pub fn test_package(pkg_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, cli_profile: Option<&str>) -> Result<()> {
    37→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    38→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
    39→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
    40→
    41→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
    42→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
    43→
    44→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
    45→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
    46→
    47→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
    48→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
    49→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
    50→
    51→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo test
    52→    let (status, spec_warnings) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    53→        let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    54→        let spec_name = spec_name_from_path(path);
    55→        let expanded_td = expand_target_dir(&spec, &spec_name)?;
    56→
    57→        // Validate the effective profile before invoking cargo
    58→        let effective = spec.cargo.profile.as_deref().or(cli_profile);
    59→        if let Some(profile) = effective {
    60→            validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;
    61→        }
    62→        println!("Testing {} with spec {}", pkg_name, path.display());
    63→
    64→        let spec_warnings = check_spec_misconfigurations(&pkg_name, &spec, &pkg_dir);
    65→
    66→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
    67→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    68→        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
    69→        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {
    70→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, &spec)?;
    71→        }
    72→
    73→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
    74→        if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
    75→            cmd.arg(format!("+{}", tc));
    76→        } else if requires_nightly_for_test(&spec) {
    77→            cmd.arg("+nightly");
    78→        }
    79→        cmd.arg("test");
    80→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
    81→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    82→
    83→        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs
    84→        cmd.env("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE", path.as_os_str());
    85→
    86→        apply_spec_to_command(
    87→            &mut cmd,
    88→            &spec,
    89→            &workspace,
    90→            cli_profile,
    91→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
    92→        )?;
    93→
    94→        // Append -Zpanic_abort_tests to RUSTFLAGS if needed (must come after
    95→        // apply_spec_to_command which may set RUSTFLAGS)
    96→        if needs_panic_abort_tests(&spec) {
    97→            let existing = cmd
    98→                .get_envs()
    99→                .find(|(k, _)| k == &"RUSTFLAGS")
   100→                .and_then(|(_, v)| v)
   101→                .map(|v| v.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   102→                .unwrap_or_default();
   103→            let new_flags = if existing.is_empty() {
   104→                "-Zpanic_abort_tests".to_string()
   105→            } else {
   106→                format!("{} -Zpanic_abort_tests", existing)
   107→            };
   108→            cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", new_flags);
   109→        }
   110→
   111→        (
   112→            cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo test")?,
   113→            spec_warnings,
   114→        )
   115→    } else {
   116→        // Validate CLI profile when no spec
   117→        if let Some(profile) = cli_profile {
   118→            validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;
   119→        }
   120→        println!("Testing {} (no tspec)", pkg_name);
   121→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   122→        cmd.arg("test");
   123→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   124→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   125→        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   126→            match p {
   127→                "debug" | "dev" => {}
   128→                _ => {
   129→                    cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   130→                }
   131→            }
   132→        }
   133→        (
   134→            cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo test")?,
   135→            Vec::new(),
   136→        )
   137→    };
   138→
   139→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
   140→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
   141→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
   142→    }
   143→
   144→    if !status.success() {
   145→        match &tspec_path {
   146→            Some(path) => {
   147→                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();
   148→                bail!(
   149→                    "cargo test failed for `{}` with spec {}",
   150→                    pkg_name,
   151→                    display_path
   152→                )
   153→            }
   154→            None => bail!("cargo test failed for `{}`", pkg_name),
   155→        }
   156→    }
   157→
   158→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   159→    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);
   160→    Ok(())
   161→}
   162→

     1→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
     2→use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     3→use std::fmt;
     4→use std::path::PathBuf;
     5→
     6→use crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};
     7→
     8→/// A value in the `[cargo.config]` table.
     9→/// Uses `#[serde(untagged)]` so TOML bools/ints/strings/tables are deserialized naturally.
    10→/// We avoid `toml::Value` because it contains `Float(f64)` which doesn't implement `Eq`.
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    12→#[serde(untagged)]
    13→pub enum ConfigValue {
    14→    Bool(bool),
    15→    Integer(i64),
    16→    String(String),
    17→    Table(BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>),
    18→}
    19→
    20→impl fmt::Display for ConfigValue {
    21→    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
    22→        match self {
    23→            ConfigValue::Bool(b) => write!(f, "{}", b),
    24→            ConfigValue::Integer(n) => write!(f, "{}", n),
    25→            ConfigValue::String(s) => write!(f, "\"{}\"", s),
    26→            ConfigValue::Table(map) => write!(f, "{:?}", map),
    27→        }
    28→    }
    29→}
    30→
    31→/// Flatten nested config into dotted key-value pairs for --config args.
    32→pub fn flatten_config(config: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    33→    let mut result = Vec::new();
    34→    flatten_inner(config, String::new(), &mut result);
    35→    result
    36→}
    37→
    38→fn flatten_inner(
    39→    map: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>,
    40→    prefix: String,
    41→    result: &mut Vec<(String, String)>,
    42→) {
    43→    for (key, value) in map {
    44→        let full_key = if prefix.is_empty() {
    45→            key.clone()
    46→        } else {
    47→            format!("{}.{}", prefix, key)
    48→        };
    49→        match value {
    50→            ConfigValue::Table(inner) => flatten_inner(inner, full_key, result),
    51→            other => result.push((full_key, other.to_string())),
    52→        }
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Map a profile name to the directory cargo uses in target/.
    57→/// `"dev"` → `"debug"`, `"release"` → `"release"`, custom → as-is.
    58→pub fn profile_dir_name(profile: &str) -> &str {
    59→    match profile {
    60→        "dev" => "debug",
    61→        other => other,
    62→    }
    63→}
    64→
    65→/// Cargo-specific configuration (flat struct)
    66→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    67→pub struct CargoConfig {
    68→    /// Build profile name (e.g., "debug", "release", "release-small", or any custom profile)
    69→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    70→    /// Target triple (e.g., "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl")
    71→    pub target_triple: Option<String>,
    72→    /// Custom target JSON file path
    73→    pub target_json: Option<PathBuf>,
    74→    /// Nightly-only -Z flags (e.g., ["panic-immediate-abort"])
    75→    #[serde(default)]
    76→    pub unstable: Vec<String>,
    77→    /// Custom target directory subdirectory for per-spec isolation.
    78→    /// Supports `{name}` (spec filename sans .ts.toml) and `{hash}` (8-char content hash).
    79→    pub target_dir: Option<String>,
    80→    /// Config values passed as `--config 'KEY=VALUE'` to cargo.
    81→    /// Supports both flat dotted keys and nested tables.
    82→    /// Each leaf entry becomes a separate `--config` arg.
    83→    #[serde(default)]
    84→    pub config: BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>,
    85→    /// Crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly only)
    86→    #[serde(default)]
    87→    pub build_std: Vec<String>,
    88→}
    89→
    90→/// Version script configuration for symbol visibility
    91→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    92→pub struct VersionScript {
    93→    /// Symbols to keep global (e.g., ["_start"])
    94→    pub global: Vec<String>,
    95→    /// Pattern for local symbols (typically "*")
    96→    #[serde(default = "default_local")]
    97→    pub local: String,
    98→}
    99→
   100→fn default_local() -> String {
   101→    "*".to_string()
   102→}
   103→
   104→/// Linker configuration (flat struct)
   105→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
   106→pub struct LinkerConfig {
   107→    /// Linker arguments (e.g., ["-static", "-nostdlib"])
   108→    #[serde(default)]
   109→    pub args: Vec<String>,
   110→    /// Version script for symbol visibility (enables --gc-sections optimization)
   111→    pub version_script: Option<VersionScript>,
   112→}
   113→
   114→/// A translation spec
   115→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
   116→pub struct Spec {
   117→    /// High-level panic mode (sets both cargo -Z and rustc -C flags)
   118→    pub panic: Option<PanicMode>,
   119→
   120→    /// High-level strip mode (sets rustc -C strip=)
   121→    pub strip: Option<StripMode>,
   122→
   123→    /// Rust toolchain override (e.g., "nightly", "stable", "nightly-2025-01-15", "1.75")
   124→    pub toolchain: Option<String>,
   125→
   126→    #[serde(default)]
   127→    pub cargo: CargoConfig,
   128→    /// Raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS
   129→    #[serde(default)]
   130→    pub rustflags: Vec<String>,
   131→    #[serde(default)]
   132→    pub linker: LinkerConfig,
   133→}
   134→
   135→#[cfg(test)]
   136→mod tests {
   137→    use super::*;
   138→
   139→    #[test]
   140→    fn spec_default_is_empty() {
   141→        let spec = Spec::default();
   142→        assert_eq!(spec.cargo, CargoConfig::default());
   143→        assert!(spec.rustflags.is_empty());
   144→        assert_eq!(spec.linker, LinkerConfig::default());
   145→    }

   161→
   162→    #[test]
   163→    fn flatten_config_nested() {
   164→        let config = BTreeMap::from([(
   165→            "profile".to_string(),
   166→            ConfigValue::Table(BTreeMap::from([(
   167→                "release".to_string(),
   168→                ConfigValue::Table(BTreeMap::from([
   169→                    (
   170→                        "opt-level".to_string(),
   171→                        ConfigValue::String("z".to_string()),
   172→                    ),
   173→                    ("codegen-units".to_string(), ConfigValue::Integer(1)),
   174→                ])),
   175→            )])),

   177→        let flat = flatten_config(&config);
   178→        assert!(flat.contains(&("profile.release.codegen-units".to_string(), "1".to_string())));
   179→        assert!(flat.contains(&("profile.release.opt-level".to_string(), "\"z\"".to_string())));
   180→        assert_eq!(flat.len(), 2);

     1→mod build;

    11→
    12→pub use build::BuildCmd;
    13→pub use clean::CleanCmd;
    14→pub use clippy::ClippyCmd;
    15→pub use compare::CompareCmd;
    16→pub use fmt::FmtCmd;
    17→pub use install::InstallCmd;
    18→pub use run::RunCmd;
    19→pub use test::TestCmd;
    20→pub use ts::TsCmd;
    21→pub use version::VersionCmd;

    23→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
    24→use std::ffi::OsString;
    25→use std::path::Path;
    26→use std::process::ExitCode;
    27→
    28→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_project_root, get_package_name, is_pop};
    29→
    30→/// Trait for command execution.
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→/// Resolve a `-p` argument (path or name) to the actual cargo package name.
    36→/// Returns Some(name) for a package, None if it resolves to a workspace root
    37→/// with no `[package]` section (meaning "operate on all packages").
    38→pub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    39→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    40→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)?;
    41→    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {
    42→        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),
    43→        Err(_) => Ok(None),
    44→    }
    45→}
    46→
    47→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    48→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    49→    subcommand: &str,
    50→    args: &[OsString],
    51→    project_root: &Path,
    52→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    53→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    54→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    55→    cmd.args(args);
    56→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    57→    let status = cmd
    58→        .status()
    59→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    60→    if status.success() {
    61→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    62→    } else {
    63→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    64→    }
    65→}
    66→
    67→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).
    68→/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.
    69→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    70→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    71→    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package
    72→    if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {
    73→        return None;
    74→    }
    75→    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
    76→}
    77→

     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
     6→use std::process::ExitCode;
     7→
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    11→use crate::cargo_build::build_package;
    12→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    13→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    14→use crate::run::run_binary;
    15→use crate::testing::test_package;
    16→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    17→use crate::workspace::{PackageKind, WorkspaceInfo};
    18→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    19→
    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→}
    93→
    94→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    95→///
    96→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    97→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    98→pub fn build_all(
    99→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   100→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   101→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   102→    strip: bool,
   103→    fail_fast: bool,
   104→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   105→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   106→        Some(n) => n,
   107→        None => {
   108→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   109→            return Vec::new();
   110→        }
   111→    };
   112→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   113→
   114→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   115→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   116→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   117→            continue;
   118→        }
   119→
   120→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   121→
   122→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   123→            vec![None]
   124→        } else {
   125→            specs
   126→                .into_iter()
   127→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   128→                .collect()
   129→        };
   130→
   131→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   132→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   133→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile) {
   134→                Ok(build_result) => {
   135→                    if strip
   136→                        && member.has_binary
   137→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   138→                    {
   139→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   140→                    }
   141→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   142→                    OpResult {
   143→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   144→                        spec,
   145→                        success: true,
   146→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   147→                        size,
   148→                    }
   149→                }
   150→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   151→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   152→                    spec,
   153→                    success: false,
   154→                    message: e.to_string(),
   155→                    size: None,
   156→                },
   157→            };
   158→
   159→            let failed = !result.success;
   160→            results.push(result);
   161→
   162→            if failed && fail_fast {
   163→                return results;
   164→            }
   165→        }
   166→    }
   167→
   168→    results
   169→}
   170→
   171→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   172→///
   173→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   174→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   175→pub fn run_all(
   176→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   177→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   178→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   179→    strip: bool,
   180→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   181→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   182→        Some(n) => n,
   183→        None => {
   184→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   185→            return Vec::new();
   186→        }
   187→    };
   188→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   189→
   190→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   191→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   192→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   193→            continue;
   194→        }
   195→
   196→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   197→
   198→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   199→            vec![None]
   200→        } else {
   201→            specs
   202→                .into_iter()
   203→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   204→                .collect()
   205→        };
   206→
   207→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   208→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   209→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile) {
   210→                Ok(build_result) => {
   211→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   212→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   213→                    }
   214→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   215→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   216→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   217→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   218→                            success: true,
   219→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   220→                            size: None,
   221→                        },
   222→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   223→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   224→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   225→                            success: false,
   226→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   227→                            size: None,
   228→                        },
   229→                    }
   230→                }
   231→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   232→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   233→                    spec,
   234→                    success: false,
   235→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   236→                    size: None,
   237→                },
   238→            };
   239→
   240→            results.push(result);
   241→        }
   242→    }
   243→
   244→    results
   245→}
   246→
   247→/// Test all workspace packages
   248→///
   249→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   250→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   251→pub fn test_all(
   252→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   253→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   254→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   255→    fail_fast: bool,
   256→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   257→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   258→        Some(n) => n,
   259→        None => {
   260→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   261→            return Vec::new();
   262→        }
   263→    };
   264→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   265→
   266→    // Test regular packages (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
   267→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   268→        if member.kind == PackageKind::Test {
   269→            continue; // Handle test packages separately
   270→        }
   271→
   272→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   273→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   274→            continue;
   275→        }
   276→
   277→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   278→
   279→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   280→            vec![None]
   281→        } else {
   282→            specs
   283→                .into_iter()
   284→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   285→                .collect()
   286→        };
   287→
   288→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   289→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   290→            let result = match test_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile) {
   291→                Ok(()) => OpResult {
   292→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   293→                    spec,
   294→                    success: true,
   295→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   296→                    size: None,
   297→                },
   298→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   299→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   300→                    spec,
   301→                    success: false,
   302→                    message: e.to_string(),
   303→                    size: None,
   304→                },
   305→            };
   306→
   307→            let failed = !result.success;
   308→            results.push(result);
   309→
   310→            if failed && fail_fast {
   311→                return results;
   312→            }
   313→        }
   314→    }
   315→
   316→    // Handle test packages (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
   317→    for member in workspace.test_members() {
   318→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   319→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   320→            continue;
   321→        }
   322→
   323→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   324→
   325→        // Build the test package (builds all binaries)
   326→        let build_tspec = specs.first().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
   327→        let spec = spec_label(&build_tspec);
   328→        let build_result = match build_package(&member.name, build_tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile) {
   329→            Ok(r) => r,
   330→            Err(e) => {
   331→                results.push(OpResult {
   332→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   333→                    spec,
   334→                    success: false,
   335→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   336→                    size: None,
   337→                });
   338→                if fail_fast {
   339→                    return results;
   340→                }
   341→                continue;
   342→            }
   343→        };
   344→
   345→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   346→        let profile_dir = crate::types::profile_dir_name(cli_profile.unwrap_or("debug"));
   347→        let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile_dir);
   348→
   349→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   350→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   351→            .into_iter()
   352→            .flatten()
   353→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   354→            .filter(|e| {
   355→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   356→                let is_file = e.path().is_file();
   357→                let is_test = name.ends_with("-tests");
   358→                let is_executable = e
   359→                    .metadata()
   360→                    .map(|m| m.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
   361→                    .unwrap_or(false);
   362→                is_file && is_test && is_executable
   363→            })
   364→            .collect();
   365→
   366→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {
   367→            results.push(OpResult {
   368→                name: member.name.clone(),
   369→                spec: spec.clone(),
   370→                success: false,
   371→                message: "no test binaries found".to_string(),
   372→                size: None,
   373→            });
   374→            continue;
   375→        }
   376→
   377→        for entry in test_binaries {
   378→            let path = entry.path();
   379→            let bin_name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   380→
   381→            print!("  Running {}... ", bin_name);
   382→
   383→            let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) {
   384→                Ok(exit_code) => {
   385→                    if exit_code == 0 {
   386→                        println!("ok");
   387→                        OpResult {
   388→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   389→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   390→                            success: true,
   391→                            message: "ok".to_string(),
   392→                            size: None,
   393→                        }
   394→                    } else {
   395→                        println!("FAILED (exit {})", exit_code);
   396→                        OpResult {
   397→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   398→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   399→                            success: false,
   400→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   401→                            size: None,
   402→                        }
   403→                    }
   404→                }
   405→                Err(e) => {
   406→                    println!("FAILED");
   407→                    OpResult {
   408→                        name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   409→                        spec: spec.clone(),
   410→                        success: false,
   411→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   412→                        size: None,
   413→                    }
   414→                }
   415→            };
   416→
   417→            let failed = !result.success;
   418→            results.push(result);
   419→
   420→            if failed && fail_fast {
   421→                return results;
   422→            }
   423→        }
   424→    }
   425→
   426→    results
   427→}
   428→
   429→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   430→struct SummaryRow {
   431→    name: String,
   432→    spec: String,
   433→    detail: String,
   434→}
   435→
   436→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   437→///
   438→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   439→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   440→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   441→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   442→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   443→fn print_summary_table(
   444→    ws_name: &str,
   445→    cmd: &str,
   446→    detail_header: &str,
   447→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   448→    footer: &str,
   449→) {
   450→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   451→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   452→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   453→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   454→    } else {
   455→        0
   456→    };
   457→
   458→    println!();
   459→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   460→    if has_spec {
   461→        println!(
   462→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   463→            "Package",
   464→            "Spec",
   465→            nw = max_name_len,
   466→            sw = max_spec_len
   467→        );
   468→    } else {
   469→        println!(
   470→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   471→            "Package",
   472→            width = max_name_len
   473→        );
   474→    }
   475→
   476→    for row in rows {
   477→        if has_spec {
   478→            println!(
   479→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   480→                row.name,
   481→                row.spec,
   482→                row.detail,
   483→                nw = max_name_len,
   484→                sw = max_spec_len
   485→            );
   486→        } else {
   487→            println!(
   488→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   489→                row.name,
   490→                row.detail,
   491→                width = max_name_len
   492→            );
   493→        }
   494→    }
   495→
   496→    println!();
   497→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   498→        println!("  {footer}");
   499→    }
   500→    print_hline!();
   501→    println!();
   502→}
   503→
   504→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   505→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   506→    let mut passed = 0;
   507→    let mut failed = 0;
   508→
   509→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   510→        .iter()
   511→        .map(|r| {
   512→            let detail = if r.success {
   513→                passed += 1;
   514→                "[PASS]".to_string()
   515→            } else {
   516→                failed += 1;
   517→                "[FAIL]".to_string()
   518→            };
   519→            SummaryRow {
   520→                name: r.name.clone(),
   521→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   522→                detail,
   523→            }
   524→        })
   525→        .collect();
   526→
   527→    print_summary_table(
   528→        name,
   529→        "TEST",
   530→        "Status",
   531→        &rows,
   532→        &format!("Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed"),
   533→    );
   534→
   535→    if failed > 0 {
   536→        ExitCode::from(1)
   537→    } else {
   538→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   539→    }
   540→}
   541→
   542→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   543→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   544→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   545→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   546→
   547→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   548→        .iter()
   549→        .map(|r| {
   550→            let status = if r.success {
   551→                ok_count += 1;
   552→                "[ OK ]"
   553→            } else {
   554→                failed_count += 1;
   555→                "[FAIL]"
   556→            };
   557→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   558→            SummaryRow {
   559→                name: r.name.clone(),
   560→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   561→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   562→            }
   563→        })
   564→        .collect();
   565→
   566→    print_summary_table(
   567→        name,
   568→        "BUILD",
   569→        "Status    Size",
   570→        &rows,
   571→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   572→    );
   573→
   574→    if failed_count > 0 {
   575→        ExitCode::from(1)
   576→    } else {
   577→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   578→    }
   579→}
   580→
   581→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   582→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   583→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   584→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   585→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   586→    } else {
   587→        format!("{}", bytes)
   588→    }
   589→}
   590→
   591→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   592→pub struct CompareResult {
   593→    pub op: OpResult,
   594→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   595→}
   596→
   597→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   598→///
   599→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   600→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   601→pub fn compare_all(
   602→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   603→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   604→    fail_fast: bool,
   605→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   606→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   607→        Some(n) => n,
   608→        None => {
   609→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   610→            return Vec::new();
   611→        }
   612→    };
   613→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   614→
   615→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   616→        if !member.has_binary {
   617→            continue;
   618→        }
   619→
   620→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   621→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   622→        } else {
   623→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   624→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   625→                continue;
   626→            }
   627→            resolved
   628→        };
   629→
   630→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   631→
   632→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths) {
   633→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   634→                OpResult {
   635→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   636→                    spec: String::new(),
   637→                    success: true,
   638→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   639→                    size: None,
   640→                },
   641→                spec_results,
   642→            ),
   643→            Err(e) => (
   644→                OpResult {
   645→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   646→                    spec: String::new(),
   647→                    success: false,
   648→                    message: e.to_string(),
   649→                    size: None,
   650→                },
   651→                Vec::new(),
   652→            ),
   653→        };
   654→
   655→        let failed = !op.success;
   656→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   657→
   658→        if failed && fail_fast {
   659→            break;
   660→        }
   661→    }
   662→
   663→    results
   664→}
   665→
   666→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   667→///
   668→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   669→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   670→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   671→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   672→
   673→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   674→    for result in results {
   675→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   676→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   677→        }
   678→    }
   679→
   680→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   681→    if results.len() > 1 {
   682→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   683→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   684→
   685→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   686→            .iter()
   687→            .map(|r| {
   688→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   689→                    ok_count += 1;
   690→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   691→                } else {
   692→                    failed_count += 1;
   693→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   694→                };
   695→                SummaryRow {
   696→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   697→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   698→                    detail,
   699→                }
   700→            })
   701→            .collect();
   702→
   703→        print_summary_table(
   704→            name,
   705→            "COMPARE",
   706→            "Status",
   707→            &rows,
   708→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   709→        );
   710→    }
   711→
   712→    if has_failure {
   713→        ExitCode::from(1)
   714→    } else {
   715→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   716→    }
   717→}
   718→
   719→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   720→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   721→    let mut error_count = 0;
   722→
   723→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   724→        .iter()
   725→        .map(|r| {
   726→            let detail = if r.success {
   727→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   728→                format!("{code:>4}")
   729→            } else {
   730→                error_count += 1;
   731→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   732→            };
   733→            SummaryRow {
   734→                name: r.name.clone(),
   735→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   736→                detail,
   737→            }
   738→        })
   739→        .collect();
   740→
   741→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   742→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   743→    } else {
   744→        String::new()
   745→    };
   746→
   747→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   748→
   749→    if error_count > 0 {
   750→        ExitCode::from(1)
   751→    } else {
   752→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   753→    }
   754→}

    13→/// Build package(s) with a translation spec
    14→#[derive(Args)]
    15→pub struct BuildCmd {
    16→    /// Package to build (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    17→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]

    19→    /// Package to build (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    20→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    21→    pub package: Option<String>,
    22→    /// Build all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    24→    pub workspace: bool,
    25→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to build with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    26→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]

    28→    /// Release build
    29→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    30→    pub release: bool,
    31→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    32→    #[arg(long)]

    41→
    42→impl BuildCmd {
    43→    /// Resolve the effective profile from --release / --profile flags.
    44→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    45→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    46→            Some(p)
    47→        } else if self.release {
    48→            Some("release")
    49→        } else {
    50→            None
    51→        }
    52→    }
    53→}
    54→
    55→impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    56→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    57→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    58→
    59→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    60→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    61→            None
    62→        } else {
    63→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    64→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    65→                None => current_package_name(),
    66→            }
    67→        };
    68→
    69→        match resolved {
    70→            None => {
    71→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    72→                let results = build_all(
    73→                    &workspace,
    74→                    &self.tspec,
    75→                    cli_profile,
    76→                    self.strip,
    77→                    self.fail_fast,
    78→                );
    79→                Ok(print_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    80→            }
    81→            Some(name) => {
    82→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    83→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile)?;
    84→                    if self.strip {
    85→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    86→                    }
    87→                } else {
    88→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    89→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    90→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    91→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    92→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    93→                        let result = build_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile)?;
    94→                        if self.strip {
    95→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    96→                        }
    97→                    }
    98→                }
    99→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   100→            }
   101→        }
   102→    }
   103→}

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;

     9→use crate::cargo_build::build_package;
    10→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    11→use crate::run::run_binary;
    12→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    13→
    14→/// Build and run package(s) with a translation spec
    15→#[derive(Args)]
    16→pub struct RunCmd {
    17→    /// Package to run (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    18→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    19→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    20→    /// Package to run (defaults to current directory or all apps)
    21→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→    pub package: Option<String>,
    23→    /// Run all workspace apps (even when in a package directory)
    24→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→    pub workspace: bool,
    26→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) to run with (defaults to package's tspec file)
    27→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    28→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    29→    /// Release build
    30→    #[arg(short, long, conflicts_with = "profile")]
    31→    pub release: bool,
    32→    /// Build profile (e.g., release, release-small, or any custom profile)
    33→    #[arg(long)]
    34→    pub profile: Option<String>,
    35→    /// Strip symbols from binary before running
    36→    #[arg(short, long)]
    37→    pub strip: bool,
    38→    /// Arguments to pass to the binary (after --)
    39→    #[arg(last = true)]
    40→    pub args: Vec<String>,
    41→}
    42→
    43→impl RunCmd {
    44→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    45→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    46→            Some(p)
    47→        } else if self.release {
    48→            Some("release")
    49→        } else {
    50→            None
    51→        }
    52→    }
    53→}
    54→
    55→impl Execute for RunCmd {
    56→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    57→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    58→
    59→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    60→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    61→            None
    62→        } else {
    63→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    64→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    65→                None => current_package_name(),
    66→            }
    67→        };
    68→
    69→        match resolved {
    70→            None => {
    71→                // Run all apps (args not supported for --workspace)
    72→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    73→                let results = run_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.strip);
    74→                Ok(print_run_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    75→            }
    76→            Some(name) => {
    77→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    78→                    // Build, optionally strip, then run
    79→                    let result = build_package(&name, None, cli_profile)?;
    80→                    if self.strip {
    81→                        strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    82→                    }
    83→                    let exit_code = run_binary(&result.binary_path, &self.args)?;
    84→                    std::process::exit(exit_code);
    85→                } else {
    86→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
    87→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    88→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
    89→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
    90→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    91→                        let result = build_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile)?;
    92→                        if self.strip {
    93→                            strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    94→                        }
    95→                        let exit_code = run_binary(&result.binary_path, &self.args)?;
    96→                        if exit_code != 0 {
    97→                            std::process::exit(exit_code);
    98→                        }
    99→                    }
   100→                    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   101→                }
   102→            }
   103→        }
   104→    }
   105→}
