     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};
     7→use crate::all::{compare_all, print_compare_summary};
     8→use crate::compare::{compare_specs, print_comparison};
     9→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};
    10→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    11→
    12→/// Compare specs for a package (size only)
    13→#[derive(Args)]
    14→pub struct CompareCmd {
    15→    /// Package to compare (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    16→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    17→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    18→    /// Package to compare (defaults to current directory)
    19→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    20→    pub package: Option<String>,
    21→    /// Compare all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    22→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    23→    pub workspace: bool,
    24→    /// Spec file(s) or glob pattern(s) (defaults to tspec* pattern)
    25→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec", num_args = 1..)]
    26→    pub tspec: Vec<String>,
    27→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    28→    #[arg(short, long)]
    29→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    30→}
    31→
    32→impl Execute for CompareCmd {
    33→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    34→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    35→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    36→            None
    37→        } else {
    38→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    39→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    40→                None => current_package_name(),
    41→            }
    42→        };
    43→
    44→        match resolved {
    45→            None => {
    46→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    47→                let results = compare_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, self.fail_fast);
    48→                Ok(print_compare_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
    49→            }
    50→            Some(pkg_name) => {
    51→                let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&pkg_name))?;
    52→                let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
    53→                let spec_paths = if self.tspec.is_empty() {
    54→                    find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec).unwrap_or_default()
    55→                } else {
    56→                    find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?
    57→                };
    58→                let results = compare_specs(&pkg_name, &spec_paths)?;
    59→                print_comparison(&pkg_name, &results);
    60→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    61→            }
    62→        }
    63→    }
    64→}
    65→
    66→#[cfg(test)]
    67→mod tests {
    68→    use super::*;
    69→    use crate::cli::Cli;
    70→    use clap::Parser;
    71→
    72→    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> CompareCmd {
    73→        let mut full = vec!["tspec", "compare"];
    74→        full.extend_from_slice(args);
    75→        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(full).unwrap();
    76→        match cli.command {
    77→            crate::cli::Commands::Compare(cmd) => cmd,
    78→            _ => panic!("expected Compare command"),
    79→        }
    80→    }
    81→
    82→    #[test]
    83→    fn package_optional() {
    84→        let cmd = parse(&[]);
    85→        assert!(cmd.package.is_none());
    86→    }
    87→
    88→    #[test]
    89→    fn package_explicit() {
    90→        let cmd = parse(&["-p", "myapp"]);
    91→        assert_eq!(cmd.package.as_deref(), Some("myapp"));
    92→    }
    93→
    94→    #[test]
    95→    fn tspec_empty_by_default() {

    98→    }
    99→

   104→    }
   105→
   106→    #[test]
   107→    fn tspec_multiple_values_one_flag() {
   108→        // Simulates shell-expanded glob: -t file1 file2
   109→        let cmd = parse(&["-t", "a.ts.toml", "b.ts.toml"]);
   110→        assert_eq!(cmd.tspec, vec!["a.ts.toml", "b.ts.toml"]);

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::ffi::OsString;

     6→
     7→use super::{Execute, execute_cargo_subcommand, resolve_package_arg};
     8→
     9→/// Clean build artifacts
    10→#[derive(Args)]
    11→pub struct CleanCmd {
    12→    /// Package to clean (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    13→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
    14→    pub positional: Option<String>,
    15→    /// Package to clean (defaults to entire workspace/project)
    16→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    17→    pub package: Option<String>,
    18→    /// Only clean release artifacts
    19→    #[arg(short, long)]
    20→    pub release: bool,
    21→}
    22→
    23→impl Execute for CleanCmd {
    24→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    25→        let mut args: Vec<OsString> = Vec::new();
    26→        let pkg_arg = self.positional.as_ref().or(self.package.as_ref());
    27→        if let Some(pkg) = pkg_arg
    28→            && let Some(name) = resolve_package_arg(pkg)?
    29→        {
    30→            args.push("-p".into());
    31→            args.push(name.into());
    32→        }
    33→        if self.release {
    34→            args.push("--release".into());
    35→        }
    36→        execute_cargo_subcommand("clean", &args, project_root)
    37→    }
    38→}
    39→

     9→/// Format source code
    10→#[derive(Args)]
    11→pub struct FmtCmd {
    12→    /// Package to format (name or path, e.g. "." for current dir)
    13→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]

    15→    /// Package to format (defaults to entire workspace)
    16→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    17→    pub package: Option<String>,

    22→    #[arg(short, long)]
    23→    pub check: bool,
    24→}

    27→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::Execute;
     7→use crate::find_paths::get_package_name;
     8→
     9→/// Install a package from a local path
    10→#[derive(Args)]
    11→pub struct InstallCmd {
    12→    /// Path to package (relative or absolute)
    13→    #[arg(long)]
    14→    pub path: PathBuf,
    15→    /// Force reinstall even if already installed
    16→    #[arg(short, long)]
    17→    pub force: bool,
    18→}
    19→
    20→impl Execute for InstallCmd {
    21→    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    22→        let resolved = self
    23→            .path
    24→            .canonicalize()
    25→            .with_context(|| format!("path not found: {}", self.path.display()))?;
    26→
    27→        let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    28→        cmd.arg("install").arg("--path").arg(&resolved);
    29→
    30→        // Pass package name if we can determine it (needed for workspaces)
    31→        if let Ok(name) = get_package_name(&resolved) {
    32→            cmd.arg(&name);
    33→        }
    34→
    35→        if self.force {
    36→            cmd.arg("--force");
    37→        }
    38→
    39→        let status = cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo install")?;
    40→        if !status.success() {
    41→            bail!("cargo install failed");
    42→        }
    43→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    44→    }
    45→}
    46→

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::{Args, Subcommand};
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::Execute;
     7→use crate::ts_cmd;
     8→
     9→/// Manage translation specs
    10→#[derive(Args)]
    11→pub struct TsCmd {
    12→    #[command(subcommand)]
    13→    command: TsCommands,
    14→}
    15→
    16→#[derive(Subcommand)]
    17→pub enum TsCommands {
    18→    /// List tspec files in workspace or for a specific package
    19→    List {
    20→        /// Package to list specs for (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→        package: Option<String>,
    23→        /// List all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→        all: bool,
    26→    },
    27→    /// Show a tspec's contents
    28→    Show {
    29→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    30→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    31→        package: Option<String>,
    32→        /// Show all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    33→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    34→        all: bool,
    35→        /// Tspec name (defaults to all tspec files)
    36→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    37→        tspec: Option<String>,
    38→    },
    39→    /// Show the content hash of a tspec
    40→    Hash {
    41→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    42→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    43→        package: Option<String>,
    44→        /// Hash all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    45→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    46→        all: bool,
    47→        /// Tspec name (defaults to package's tspec file)
    48→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    49→        tspec: Option<String>,
    50→    },
    51→    /// Create a new tspec file
    52→    New {
    53→        /// Name for the new tspec (default: "tspec")
    54→        #[arg(default_value = "tspec")]
    55→        name: String,
    56→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    57→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    58→        package: Option<String>,
    59→        /// Copy from existing tspec (package/spec or just spec name in same package)
    60→        #[arg(short = 'f', long = "from")]
    61→        from: Option<String>,
    62→    },
    63→    /// Set a field in a tspec (scalar value or replace entire array)
    64→    ///
    65→    /// For scalars: tspec ts set key value
    66→    /// For arrays: tspec ts set key val1 val2 ...
    67→    Set {
    68→        /// Field key (e.g., "cargo.profile", "linker.args")
    69→        key: String,
    70→        /// Value(s). For scalars, one value. For arrays, each arg is an element.
    71→        #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
    72→        value: Vec<String>,
    73→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    74→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    75→        package: Option<String>,
    76→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    77→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    78→        tspec: Option<String>,
    79→    },
    80→    /// Remove a field from a tspec (preserves comments)
    81→    Unset {
    82→        /// Key to remove (e.g., "cargo.profile", "panic", "linker.args")
    83→        key: String,
    84→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    85→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    86→        package: Option<String>,
    87→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    88→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    89→        tspec: Option<String>,
    90→    },
    91→    /// Add items to an array field (append by default, or insert at position)
    92→    Add {
    93→        /// Field key (must be an array field, e.g., "linker.args")
    94→        key: String,
    95→        /// Items to add
    96→        #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
    97→        value: Vec<String>,
    98→        /// Insert at this index instead of appending
    99→        #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   100→        index: Option<usize>,
   101→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   102→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   103→        package: Option<String>,
   104→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   105→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   106→        tspec: Option<String>,
   107→    },
   108→    /// Remove items from an array field (by value or by index)
   109→    Remove {
   110→        /// Field key (must be an array field, e.g., "linker.args")
   111→        key: String,
   112→        /// Items to remove by value (not used with --index)
   113→        #[arg(allow_hyphen_values = true)]
   114→        value: Vec<String>,
   115→        /// Remove item at this index instead of by value
   116→        #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   117→        index: Option<usize>,
   118→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   119→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   120→        package: Option<String>,
   121→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   122→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   123→        tspec: Option<String>,
   124→    },
   125→    /// Create a versioned backup of a tspec
   126→    Backup {
   127→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   128→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   129→        package: Option<String>,
   130→        /// Tspec to backup (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
   131→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   132→        tspec: Option<String>,
   133→    },
   134→    /// Restore a tspec from a versioned backup
   135→    Restore {
   136→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
   137→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   138→        package: Option<String>,
   139→        /// Backup tspec to restore from (required, e.g., "t2-001-abcd1234.ts.toml")
   140→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   141→        tspec: String,
   142→    },
   143→}
   144→
   145→impl Execute for TsCmd {
   146→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   147→        match &self.command {
   148→            TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
   149→                ts_cmd::list_tspecs(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all)?;
   150→            }
   151→            TsCommands::Show {
   152→                package,
   153→                all,
   154→                tspec,
   155→            } => {
   156→                ts_cmd::show_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   157→            }
   158→            TsCommands::Hash {
   159→                package,
   160→                all,
   161→                tspec,
   162→            } => {
   163→                ts_cmd::hash_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   164→            }
   165→            TsCommands::New {
   166→                name,
   167→                package,
   168→                from,
   169→            } => {
   170→                ts_cmd::new_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), name, from.as_deref())?;
   171→            }
   172→            TsCommands::Set {
   173→                key,
   174→                value,
   175→                package,
   176→                tspec,
   177→            } => {
   178→                ts_cmd::set_value(
   179→                    project_root,
   180→                    package.as_deref(),
   181→                    key,
   182→                    value,
   183→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   184→                )?;
   185→            }
   186→            TsCommands::Unset {
   187→                key,
   188→                package,
   189→                tspec,
   190→            } => {
   191→                ts_cmd::unset_value(project_root, package.as_deref(), key, tspec.as_deref())?;
   192→            }
   193→            TsCommands::Add {
   194→                key,
   195→                value,
   196→                index,
   197→                package,
   198→                tspec,
   199→            } => {
   200→                ts_cmd::add_value(
   201→                    project_root,
   202→                    package.as_deref(),
   203→                    key,
   204→                    value,
   205→                    *index,
   206→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   207→                )?;
   208→            }
   209→            TsCommands::Remove {
   210→                key,
   211→                value,
   212→                index,
   213→                package,
   214→                tspec,
   215→            } => {
   216→                ts_cmd::remove_value(
   217→                    project_root,
   218→                    package.as_deref(),
   219→                    key,
   220→                    value,
   221→                    *index,
   222→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   223→                )?;
   224→            }
   225→            TsCommands::Backup { package, tspec } => {
   226→                ts_cmd::backup_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec.as_deref())?;
   227→            }
   228→            TsCommands::Restore { package, tspec } => {
   229→                ts_cmd::restore_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec)?;
   230→            }
   231→        }
   232→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   233→    }
   234→}
   235→
```

     1→use std::path::Path;
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→
     5→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
     6→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, plain_cargo_build_release};
     7→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
     8→
     9→/// Result of building a spec
    10→pub struct SpecResult {
    11→    pub name: String,
    12→    pub size: u64,
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Compare multiple specs for a package
    16→pub fn compare_specs(
    17→    pkg_name: &str,
    18→    spec_paths: &[impl AsRef<Path> + std::fmt::Debug],
    19→) -> Result<Vec<SpecResult>> {
    20→    println!("Comparing {} builds:\n", pkg_name);
    21→
    22→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    23→
    24→    // Always build cargo --release baseline first (unstripped + stripped)
    25→    match build_baseline(pkg_name) {
    26→        Ok((size, stripped_size)) => {
    27→            results.push(SpecResult {
    28→                name: "cargo --release".to_string(),
    29→                size,
    30→            });
    31→            results.push(SpecResult {
    32→                name: "cargo --release-strip".to_string(),
    33→                size: stripped_size,
    34→            });
    35→        }
    36→        Err(_) => {
    37→            println!("    baseline build failed, skipping");
    38→        }
    39→    }
    40→    println!();
    41→
    42→    for spec_path in spec_paths {
    43→        let spec_path = spec_path.as_ref();
    44→        let name = spec_path
    45→            .file_name()
    46→            .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    47→            .unwrap_or_else(|| spec_path.display().to_string());
    48→
    49→        let size = build_spec(pkg_name, spec_path)?;
    50→        results.push(SpecResult { name, size });
    51→        println!();
    52→    }
    53→
    54→    // Sort by size (smallest first)
    55→    results.sort_by_key(|r| r.size);
    56→
    57→    Ok(results)
    58→}
    59→
    60→/// Build baseline and return (unstripped_size, stripped_size)
    61→fn build_baseline(pkg_name: &str) -> Result<(u64, u64)> {
    62→    println!("  cargo --release:");
    63→
    64→    let build_result = plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name)?;
    65→
    66→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    67→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    68→
    69→    strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    70→    let stripped_size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    71→
    72→    Ok((size, stripped_size))
    73→}
    74→
    75→fn build_spec(pkg_name: &str, spec_path: &Path) -> Result<u64> {
    76→    let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    77→    println!(
    78→        "  {}:",
    79→        spec_path.file_name().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy()
    80→    );
    81→
    82→    // Build using spec settings (profile, strip, etc. are all in the spec)
    83→    let build_result = build_package(pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), None)?;
    84→
    85→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    86→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    87→
    88→    Ok(size)
    89→}
    90→
    91→pub fn print_comparison(pkg_name: &str, results: &[SpecResult]) {
    92→    let largest_size = results.iter().map(|r| r.size).max().unwrap_or(0);
    93→    let max_name_len = results.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(4);
    94→
    95→    // Format percent change: show reduction with minus sign, baseline as 0.0%
    96→    let fmt_pct = |size: u64| -> String {
    97→        if largest_size == 0 {
    98→            return "   0.0%".to_string();
    99→        }
   100→        let pct = ((largest_size as f64 - size as f64) / largest_size as f64) * 100.0;
   101→        if pct > 0.0 {
   102→            format!("{:>7.1}%", -pct)
   103→        } else {
   104→            "   0.0%".to_string()
   105→        }
   106→    };
   107→
   108→    println!();
   109→    print_header!(format!("{} COMPARE SUMMARY", pkg_name));
   110→    println!(
   111→        "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {:>8}",
   112→        "Spec",
   113→        "Size",
   114→        "Change",
   115→        width = max_name_len
   116→    );
   117→    for result in results {
   118→        println!(
   119→            "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {}",
   120→            result.name,
   121→            format_size(result.size),
   122→            fmt_pct(result.size),
   123→            width = max_name_len
   124→        );
   125→    }
   126→    print_hline!();
   127→    println!();
   128→}
   129→
   130→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   131→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   132→        format!("{:.3}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   133→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   134→        format!("{:.3}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   135→    } else {
   136→        format!("{}", bytes)
   137→    }
   138→}
   139→

     1→//! High-level build options that expand to lower-level flags.
     2→//!
     3→//! These provide "easy generality" - user picks intent, implementation
     4→//! details (which cargo/rustc flags to set) are handled automatically.
     5→
     6→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
     7→
     8→/// Panic handling strategy.
     9→///
    10→/// This is a high-level option that automatically sets the appropriate
    11→/// cargo and rustc flags.
    12→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    13→#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
    14→pub enum PanicMode {
    15→    /// Default Rust behavior - panic unwinds the stack.
    16→    #[default]
    17→    Unwind,
    18→
    19→    /// Panic aborts immediately (no unwinding).
    20→    /// Sets: rustc -C panic=abort
    21→    Abort,
    22→
    23→    /// Panic aborts with no formatting overhead (nightly only).
    24→    /// Sets: cargo -Z panic-immediate-abort AND rustc -C panic=immediate-abort
    25→    /// This eliminates all panic formatting machinery for smallest binaries.
    26→    ImmediateAbort,
    27→}
    28→
    29→impl PanicMode {
    30→    /// Returns true if this mode requires nightly toolchain.
    31→    pub fn requires_nightly(&self) -> bool {
    32→        matches!(self, PanicMode::ImmediateAbort)
    33→    }
    34→
    35→    /// Returns the cargo -Z flag if needed.
    36→    pub fn cargo_z_flag(&self) -> Option<&'static str> {
    37→        match self {
    38→            PanicMode::ImmediateAbort => Some("panic-immediate-abort"),
    39→            _ => None,
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→
    43→    /// Returns the rustc -C panic= value.
    44→    pub fn rustc_panic_value(&self) -> Option<&'static str> {
    45→        match self {
    46→            PanicMode::Unwind => None, // default, no flag needed
    47→            PanicMode::Abort => Some("abort"),
    48→            PanicMode::ImmediateAbort => Some("immediate-abort"),
    49→        }
    50→    }
    51→}
    52→
    53→/// Symbol stripping mode.
    54→///
    55→/// This is a high-level option that sets the rustc strip flag.
    56→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    57→#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
    58→pub enum StripMode {
    59→    /// No stripping (default).
    60→    #[default]
    61→    None,
    62→
    63→    /// Strip debug info only.
    64→    /// Sets: rustc -C strip=debuginfo
    65→    Debuginfo,
    66→
    67→    /// Strip all symbols.
    68→    /// Sets: rustc -C strip=symbols
    69→    Symbols,
    70→}
    71→
    72→impl StripMode {
    73→    /// Returns the rustc -C strip= value, if any.
    74→    pub fn rustc_strip_value(&self) -> Option<&'static str> {
    75→        match self {
    76→            StripMode::None => None,
    77→            StripMode::Debuginfo => Some("debuginfo"),
    78→            StripMode::Symbols => Some("symbols"),
    79→        }
    80→    }
    81→}
    82→
    83→#[cfg(test)]
    84→mod tests {
    85→    use super::*;
    86→
    87→    #[test]
    88→    fn unwind_is_default() {
    89→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::default(), PanicMode::Unwind);
    90→    }
    91→
    92→    #[test]
    93→    fn only_immediate_abort_requires_nightly() {
    94→        assert!(!PanicMode::Unwind.requires_nightly());
    95→        assert!(!PanicMode::Abort.requires_nightly());
    96→        assert!(PanicMode::ImmediateAbort.requires_nightly());
    97→    }
    98→
    99→    #[test]
   100→    fn cargo_z_flag_only_for_immediate_abort() {
   101→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::Unwind.cargo_z_flag(), None);
   102→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::Abort.cargo_z_flag(), None);
   103→        assert_eq!(
   104→            PanicMode::ImmediateAbort.cargo_z_flag(),
   105→            Some("panic-immediate-abort")
   106→        );
   107→    }
   108→
   109→    #[test]
   110→    fn rustc_panic_values() {
   111→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::Unwind.rustc_panic_value(), None);
   112→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::Abort.rustc_panic_value(), Some("abort"));
   113→        assert_eq!(
   114→            PanicMode::ImmediateAbort.rustc_panic_value(),
   115→            Some("immediate-abort")
   116→        );
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    #[test]
   120→    fn strip_none_is_default() {
   121→        assert_eq!(StripMode::default(), StripMode::None);
   122→    }
   123→
   124→    #[test]

   128→        assert_eq!(StripMode::Symbols.rustc_strip_value(), Some("symbols"));
   129→    }
   130→}

     1→# Chores-5
     2→
     3→## 20260216 - Future: config_key_value and rustc field migration
     4→
     5→### Context
     6→
     7→`[cargo.config_key_value]` is now implemented, providing a general-purpose mechanism for
     8→passing `--config KEY=VALUE` args to cargo. This enables per-package profile overrides
     9→without bleeding settings through RUSTFLAGS.
    10→
    11→### Open questions
    12→
    13→- **Auto-scoping:** Should tspec automatically scope config keys with
    14→  `profile.*.package.<name>` so users don't have to write the full path? Currently users
    15→  write exact cargo config keys — no magic.
    16→- **`[rustc]` field migration:** Several `[rustc]` fields (`opt_level`, `lto`,
    17→  `codegen_units`) duplicate what cargo profiles express. These could migrate to
    18→  `config_key_value` entries, but that's a separate follow-up. The existing fields work
    19→  fine via RUSTFLAGS for now.
    20→- **High-level fields (`panic`, `strip`):** These expand to both cargo and rustc flags.
    21→  Moving them to `config_key_value` would lose the multi-flag expansion. They likely stay
    22→  as top-level fields.
    23→
    24→### Finding: RUSTFLAGS vs --config produce same-size but non-identical binaries
    25→
    26→Tested with `tspec-build` library crate using two equivalent specs:
    27→- `tspec.opt.ts.toml` — uses `rustc.opt_level = "z"` and `rustc.codegen_units = 1` (RUSTFLAGS)
    28→- `tspec.opt-kv.ts.toml` — uses `[cargo.config_key_value]` with equivalent `--config` args
    29→
    30→Results:
    31→- Binary sizes: **identical** (1,117,880 bytes both)
    32→- Binary content: **differs** (~72K of ~1.1M bytes, ~6.5%)
    33→- Changing `target_dir` alone does NOT cause differences (confirmed byte-identical)
    34→- The difference is solely from RUSTFLAGS `-C opt-level=z` vs `--config 'profile.release.opt-level="z"'`
    35→
    36→The two mechanisms produce equivalently optimized but not bit-identical binaries.
    37→Cause is unclear — possibly different flag ordering, different cargo-internal rustc
    38→invocation paths, or non-deterministic codegen. Not a practical concern since the
    39→optimization effect is the same.
    40→
    41→### Status
    42→
    43→Done — see next section.
    44→
    45→## 20260216 - Add glob support for `-t` flag on build, run, test
    46→
    47→### Context
    48→
    49→The compare command already supported glob patterns for `-t` and `-w`/`--workspace` for
    50→all-packages mode. Build, run, and test commands only accepted a single `-t` value and
    51→had no `-w` flag.
    52→
    53→### Changes
    54→
    55→- Changed `-t`/`--tspec` from `Option<String>` to `Vec<String>` with `num_args=1..`
    56→  on build, run, and test commands, matching compare's pattern
    57→- When multiple specs match a glob, the operation executes once per spec in sequence
    58→- Block `-t` in all-packages mode (`-w`) with a clear error
    59→- Added clap parsing tests to all three commands
    60→
    61→### Status
    62→
    63→Done — released in v0.11.3.
    64→
    65→## 20260216 - Remove `rustc.opt_level`, `rustc.lto`, `rustc.codegen_units` — migrate to `config_key_value`
    66→
    67→### Context
    68→
    69→These three `[rustc]` fields duplicate what `[cargo.config_key_value]` can express via
    70→`cargo --config`. We confirmed they produce identical binary sizes. Since we're the only
    71→users, we can remove them without deprecation. `[rustc]` section stays with `build_std`
    72→and `flags`.
    73→
    74→### Changes
    75→
    76→1. **types.rs:** Deleted `OptLevel` enum; removed `opt_level`, `lto`, `codegen_units`
    77→   from `RustcConfig` (kept `build_std`, `flags`)
    78→2. **cargo_build.rs:** Removed RUSTFLAGS generation for the three fields; removed
    79→   `OptLevel` import
    80→3. **ts_cmd/edit.rs:** Removed 3 `FIELD_REGISTRY` entries, 3 `validate_value` arms,
    81→   `parse_scalar_value` special cases; updated/deleted affected unit tests
    82→4. **ts_cmd/set.rs:** Deleted `set_rustc_lto`, `set_rustc_opt_level`, `set_codegen_units` tests
    83→5. **ts_cmd/unset.rs:** Rewrote `unset_nested_field` test to use `rustc.build_std`/`flags`
    84→6. **cmd/ts.rs:** Changed `"rustc.lto"` doc examples to `"cargo.profile"`
    85→7. **Spec files:** Migrated `opt_level`/`codegen_units` to `[cargo.config_key_value]`
    86→   in `tspec.release.ts.toml`, `tspec.musl.ts.toml`, `tspec-build/tspec.opt.ts.toml`
    87→8. **Test fixtures:** Migrated `tests/data/minimal.toml`; updated `tests/tspec_test.rs`
    88→   `load_minimal_spec` to check `config_key_value` instead of `rustc.opt_level`
    89→
    90→### Status
    91→
    92→Done.
    93→
    94→## 20260217 - Fix: test race condition with `std::env::set_var` / `remove_var`
    95→
    96→### Problem
    97→
    98→`tspec test` intermittently failed on the `resolve_spec_path_none_with_env` test in
    99→`tspec-build`. The failure was not reproducible when running `tspec test -p tspec-build`
   100→alone — only when both packages ran together via `tspec test`.
   101→
   102→### Root cause
   103→
   104→Rust runs tests in parallel on multiple threads within the same process.
   105→`std::env::set_var()` and `std::env::remove_var()` are process-wide — they mutate
   106→shared global state. Two tests that manipulate the same env var (`TSPEC_SPEC_FILE`)
   107→race each other:
   108→
   109→- `resolve_spec_path_none_with_env` — sets `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE`, calls function, removes it
   110→- `resolve_spec_path_none_without_env` — removes `TSPEC_SPEC_FILE`, asserts `None`
   111→
   112→If the "without" test runs between the set and remove of the "with" test, it sees the
   113→var and fails. Or vice versa. This is a well-known Rust testing pitfall — `set_var` is
   114→marked `unsafe` in Rust 2024 edition precisely because of this.
   115→
   116→### Solutions considered
   117→
   118→1. **Static Mutex** — wrap env-var tests so they run one at a time. Works but easy to
   119→   forget for new tests, and doesn't prevent the class of bug.
   120→2. **`serial_test` crate** — adds `#[serial]` attribute. Clean syntax but adds a
   121→   dependency and still relies on developers remembering to annotate.
   122→3. **`--test-threads=1`** — heavy-handed, slows down all tests.
   123→4. **`_inner` pattern (chosen)** — extract a pure function that takes env values as
   124→   parameters. The public function reads env vars and delegates. Tests call `_inner`
   125→   directly with their own values — no shared state, no race, impossible to flake.
   126→5. **Temp directory copies (for future integration tests)** — when tests need to
   127→   modify real package layouts (Cargo.toml, spec files, source), each test copies
   128→   the fixture into its own `tempdir()`. No shared filesystem state, parallel-safe,
   129→   cleanup is automatic via `Drop`.
   130→
   131→### Principle
   132→
   133→Push side effects (env var reads, file I/O) to the edges. Keep core logic pure and
   134→parameterized. Tests exercise the logic without touching the real world. For filesystem
   135→mutations, each test owns a private copy.
   136→
   137→### Changes
   138→
   139→- **tspec-build/src/lib.rs:** Split `resolve_spec_path` into a thin env-reading wrapper
   140→  and `resolve_spec_path_inner` that takes `manifest_dir` and `env_spec_file` as
   141→  `Option<&str>` parameters. Rewrote all three `resolve_spec_path_*` tests and
   142→  `emit_from_reads_spec_file` to call `_inner` directly — no `set_var`/`remove_var`.
   143→
   144→### Status
   145→
   146→Done.
   147→
   148→## 20260217 - Refactor cargo runner functions into unified fn
   149→
   150→### Context
   151→
   152→Three functions share nearly identical structure:
   153→- `cargo_build::build_package` — builds with optional spec, returns `BuildResult`
   154→- `testing::test_package` — tests with optional spec, returns `()`
   155→- `cargo_build::plain_cargo_build_release` — builds without spec, returns `BuildResult`
   156→
   157→They all follow the same skeleton: find workspace/package/tspec, clean stale build.rs,
   158→optionally load spec, generate build.rs if needed, construct `Command` (with optional
   159→`+nightly`), run, clean up, warn about stale files.
   160→
   161→`plain_cargo_build_release` is just `build_package` with `tspec=None` and `release=true`.
   162→`test_package` differs only in the subcommand (`test` vs `build`) and has its own
   163→duplicate `requires_nightly()` function in `testing.rs`.
   164→
   165→### Plan
   166→
   167→Unify into a single `run_cargo` function that takes the subcommand as a parameter.
   168→`plain_cargo_build_release` becomes a thin call. `test_package` becomes a wrapper that
   169→calls the unified function and discards `BuildResult`. The duplicate `requires_nightly()`
   170→in `testing.rs` is eliminated.
   171→
   172→### Differences to reconcile
   173→
   174→| Aspect | `build_package` | `test_package` | `plain_cargo_build_release` |
   175→|---|---|---|---|
   176→| Subcommand | `build` | `test` | `build` |
   177→| Spec | optional | optional | never |
   178→| Returns | `BuildResult` | `()` | `BuildResult` |
   179→| `+nightly` | via `build_cargo_command()` | inlined `requires_nightly()` | no (no spec) |
   180→| build.rs gen | yes (checks bin target) | yes (no bin check) | no |
   181→
   182→### Status
   183→
   184→Not started.
   185→
   186→## 20260217 - Remove `[rustc]` section, promote `build_std`, add `rustflags`
   187→
   188→### Context
   189→
   190→The `[rustc]` section has two remaining fields: `build_std` (actually a cargo `-Z` flag,
   191→not a rustc flag) and `flags` (raw RUSTFLAGS passthrough). `build_std` belongs under
   192→`[cargo]` since it triggers `+nightly` and `-Z build-std=...`. `flags` becomes a
   193→top-level `rustflags` array — a simple escape hatch mirroring `RUSTFLAGS` semantics.
   194→
   195→### Before → After
   196→
   197→```toml
   198→# Before
   199→[rustc]
   200→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]
   201→flags = ["-C", "some-thing"]
   202→
   203→# After
   204→[cargo]
   205→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]
   206→
   207→rustflags = ["-C", "some-thing"]
   208→```
   209→
   210→### Changes
   211→
   212→1. **types.rs:** Move `build_std` to `CargoConfig`, add `rustflags` to `Spec`, delete `RustcConfig`
   213→2. **cargo_build.rs:** Update all `spec.rustc` references to new locations
   214→3. **testing.rs:** Update duplicate `requires_nightly()` to use `spec.cargo.build_std`
   215→4. **ts_cmd/edit.rs:** Update field registry and tests
   216→5. **ts_cmd/set.rs, unset.rs:** Update tests
   217→6. **tspec.rs:** Update tests (remove `RustcConfig` references)
   218→7. **tests/tspec_test.rs:** Update integration tests
   219→8. **README.md, CLAUDE.md:** Update documentation

   221→### Status
   222→
   223→Done — released in v0.11.6. Also renamed `config_key_value` to `config` with nested table support (dev2).
   224→
   225→## 20260218 - Add custom profile support
   226→
   227→### Context
   228→
   229→Currently `cargo.profile` only accepts `"debug"` or `"release"` (a Rust enum). Cargo supports
   230→custom profiles defined in `Cargo.toml` (e.g., `[profile.release-small]` with `inherits = "release"`),
   231→selected via `cargo build --profile <name>`. tspec should support selecting and overriding these
   232→custom profiles.
   233→
   234→Key insight: Custom profile **definitions** must live in `Cargo.toml` (cargo requirement — they
   235→need `inherits`). tspec's role is **selecting** a profile and **overriding** its settings via
   236→`cargo.config`.
   237→
   238→Cargo's profile-to-directory mapping: `dev` → `target/debug/`, `release` → `target/release/`,
   239→custom → `target/<name>/`.
   240→
   241→### Plan
   242→
   243→Multi-step (`-devN` series), version `0.12.0`.
   244→
   245→**Step 1 (dev1):** Core type change — replace `Profile` enum with `String`, update command
   246→generation (`"debug"`/`"dev"` → no flag, anything else → `--profile <name>`), update path
   247→resolution (`profile_dir_name()` helper), relax validation.
   248→
   249→**Step 2 (dev2):** CLI `--profile <name>` flag on build/run/test commands, thread through
   250→call chain, replace `release: bool` with `Option<String>`.
   251→
   252→**Step 3:** Final release — remove `-dev` suffix.
   253→
   254→### Changes
   255→
   256→**Step 1 (dev1):** Replaced `enum Profile { Debug, Release }` with `Option<String>`.
   257→Updated `apply_spec_to_command()` to emit `--profile <name>` for any non-debug/dev profile.
   258→Added `profile_dir_name()` helper for `dev` → `debug` directory mapping.
   259→Removed `validate_config_profiles()` — custom profiles in `[cargo.config]` are now accepted.
   260→Relaxed `validate_value("cargo.profile", ...)` to accept any string.
   261→
   262→**Step 2 (dev2):** Added `--profile <name>` flag to build/run/test commands (`conflicts_with = "release"`).
   263→Replaced `release: bool` with `cli_profile: Option<&str>` throughout the call chain:
   264→`build_package()`, `test_package()`, `apply_spec_to_command()`, `get_binary_path()`,
   265→`get_binary_path_simple()`, `build_all()`, `test_all()`, `run_all()`.
   266→Simplified `plain_cargo_build_release()` to a thin wrapper: `build_package(name, None, Some("release"))`.
   267→Added `validate_profile()` to catch undefined custom profiles early with a clear error
   268→message instead of cargo's confusing "missing inherits" error.
   269→Added `[profile.other]` to `Cargo.toml` and `tspec.other.ts.toml` for real custom profile testing.
   270→
   271→### Status
   272→
   273→Done — released in v0.12.0.
   274→
   275→## 20260218 - Allow -t glob patterns in all-packages mode
   276→
   277→### Context
   278→
   279→Currently `-t`/`--tspec` only works in single-package mode. In all-packages mode (pure workspace
   280→or `--workspace`), using `-t` bails with "requires a package target". The user wants `-t tspec*`
   281→to apply to all sub-packages in a workspace, resolving the glob per-package and skipping packages
   282→with no matches.
   283→
   284→### Plan
   285→
   286→**Step 1 (v0.12.5-dev1):** Update `all.rs` batch functions

   288→- Change `build_all`, `test_all`, `run_all` signatures from `tspec: Option<&str>` to `tspec_patterns: &[String]`
   289→- Add `tspec_patterns: &[String]` parameter to `compare_all`

   304→## 20260218 - Refactor summary printers

   306→### Context

   330→## 20260219 - Add toolchain field
   331→
   332→### Context
   333→
   334→When testing packages in rlibc-x that use `-Z build-std` (nightly-only), tspec auto-detects
   335→the need for nightly and hardcodes `+nightly`. A `toolchain` field lets specs explicitly
   336→declare which toolchain to use (e.g., `nightly`, `stable`, `nightly-2025-01-15`, `1.75`),
   337→giving users control and making specs self-documenting. When set, it overrides auto-detection;
   338→when absent, current behavior is preserved.
   339→

   344→- `src/testing.rs` — `test_package()` checks `spec.toolchain` first, falls back to `requires_nightly_for_test()`

```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options. Each tspec applies to a Cargo **package** — the unit with a `Cargo.toml`. If different crates need different compilation settings, they should be in separate packages.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects (i.e. Plain Old Packages, POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Contributing
    19→
    20→**All non-trivial changes must be on a feature branch** — never commit directly to `main`. This applies even for solo development.
    21→
    22→```bash
    23→git checkout -b feat-my-feature    # Create a branch
    24→# ... make changes, commit ...
    25→git checkout main                  # Switch back
    26→git merge --no-ff feat-my-feature  # Merge with merge commit (or squash-merge)
    27→git push
    28→```
    29→
    30→Branch naming: `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`.
    31→
    32→**Protect main on GitHub:** Enable branch protection (Settings > Branches) to block force pushes and optionally require PRs.
    33→
    34→For the full feature workflow (version bumps, notes, merge steps, Claude Code conventions), see [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#feature-workflow).
    35→
    36→## Usage
    37→
    38→```bash
    39→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    40→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    41→tspec build myapp                          # Same, using positional argument
    42→tspec build .                              # Build the package in current directory
    43→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    44→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    45→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbols with tspec.ts.toml if present
    46→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    47→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    48→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    49→tspec build -t 'tspec*'                    # Build all packages using matching specs (quote the glob!)
    50→tspec build -t 'tspec-opt*'               # Build all packages with tspec-opt specs only
    51→tspec compare -p myapp                     # Compare binary sizes for a package
    52→tspec compare -p myapp -t *.ts.toml        # Compare using shell-expanded glob
    53→tspec compare                              # Compare all packages (workspace mode)
    54→tspec compare -w                           # Force workspace mode from inside a package
    55→tspec compare -w -f                        # Workspace mode, stop on first failure
    56→```
    57→
    58→The `-p` flag or positional argument specifies a package by name or path (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages). Paths like `.` are resolved to the actual cargo package name. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` means "all packages."
    59→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    60→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically. In workspace mode (no `-p`, or with `-w`), `-t` patterns are resolved per-package — each sub-package is searched for matching specs, and packages with no matches are skipped.
    61→
    62→**Important: quote glob patterns with `-t`.** The shell expands unquoted globs *before* tspec sees them. At a workspace root, `tspec build -t tspec*` may expand to literal filenames from the root directory (or worse, match `target/`), rather than being matched per-package. Always quote: `-t 'tspec*'`. tspec will warn if it detects shell-expanded non-tspec arguments.
    63→
    64→**Note on `cmd` vs `cmd .`:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), while `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata files. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    65→
    66→## tspec Files
    67→
    68→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    69→
    70→```toml
    71→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    72→
    73→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    74→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    75→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    76→toolchain = "nightly"                      # override toolchain (e.g., "nightly", "stable", "1.75")
    77→rustflags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS
    78→
    79→[cargo]
    80→profile = "release"                        # "debug" (default), "release", or custom profile
    81→target_triple = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"
    82→target_json = "path/to/custom-target.json" # custom target spec (auto-adds -Z json-target-spec)
    83→unstable = ["panic-immediate-abort"]       # -Z flags (nightly only)
    84→target_dir = "{name}"                      # per-spec target dir; supports {name} and {hash} placeholders
    85→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)
    86→
    87→[cargo.config.profile.release]             # nested tables under [cargo.config] become individual
    88→opt-level = "z"                            # --config KEY=VALUE args to cargo; any valid cargo
    89→lto = true                                 # config key works (profile, build, target, etc.)
    90→codegen-units = 1
    91→
    92→[linker]
    93→args = ["-static", "-nostartfiles"]
    94→
    95→[linker.version_script]                    # symbol visibility control (enables --gc-sections)
    96→global = ["_start"]                        # symbols to keep
    97→local = "*"                                # default: "*" (hide everything else)
    98→```
    99→
   100→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically.
   101→
   102→### Key Concepts
   103→
   104→- **One tspec per package** - A tspec applies to all crates (targets) within a Cargo package. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
   105→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
   106→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
   107→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs; always shows both `cargo --release` and `cargo --release-strip` baselines. Supports `-w` for all-packages mode — per-package tables are collected and printed together at the end. With a single package, only its comparison table is shown
   108→
   109→### How Linker Flags Work
   110→
   111→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
   112→
   113→```rust
   114→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
   115→fn main() {
   116→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
   117→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
   118→}
   119→```
   120→
   121→The only other alternative is to add these linker flags to RUSTFLAGS, but that
   122→affects ALL compilations (including dependency build scripts), causing crashes
   123→with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated build.rs scopes flags to just
   124→the binary.
   125→
   126→### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)
   127→
   128→The `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.
   129→
   130→**Profile settings** are the most common use case:
   131→
   132→```toml
   133→[cargo.config.profile.release]
   134→opt-level = "z"
   135→codegen-units = 1
   136→lto = true
   137→```
   138→
   139→This produces: `cargo --config profile.release.opt-level="z" --config profile.release.codegen-units=1 --config profile.release.lto=true`
   140→
   141→**Other cargo config keys** work too:
   142→
   143→```toml
   144→[cargo.config.target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu]
   145→linker = "clang"
   146→runner = "qemu-x86_64"
   147→```
   148→
   149→**Note:** Do not use `build.rustflags` in `[cargo.config]`. tspec sets the `RUSTFLAGS` env var (from top-level `rustflags`, `panic`, and `strip`), which takes precedence over `--config build.rustflags` per cargo's precedence rules. Use the top-level `rustflags` array instead.
   150→
   151→**Custom profiles:** Any profile defined in your workspace `Cargo.toml` (with `inherits`) can be used. tspec validates that custom profiles exist before invoking cargo. Use `cargo.profile` in the spec or `--profile` on the CLI.
   152→
   153→**Flat dotted keys** also work — TOML treats them equivalently:
   154→
   155→```toml
   156→[cargo.config]
   157→"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"
   158→```
   159→
   160→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
   161→
   162→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing. All commands that modify files preserve comments and formatting.
   163→
   164→| Command | Description |
   165→|---------|-------------|
   166→| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |
   167→| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |
   168→| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |
   169→| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |
   170→| `ts set` | Set a scalar value or replace an entire array |
   171→| `ts unset` | Remove a field (scalar or array) from a tspec entirely |
   172→| `ts add` | Add items to an array field (append or insert at position) |
   173→| `ts remove` | Remove items from an array field (by value or by index) |
   174→| `ts backup` | Create a versioned backup copy (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) |
   175→| `ts restore` | Restore a tspec by copying a versioned backup back to its original name |
   176→
   177→```bash
   178→# List / show / hash
   179→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   180→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List tspec files for a package
   181→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents
   182→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   183→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   184→
   185→# Create new tspec files
   186→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml with defaults
   187→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   188→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec (byte-for-byte)
   189→
   190→# Set scalar values
   191→tspec ts set strip symbols -p myapp              # Set a scalar field
   192→tspec ts set cargo.profile release               # No quoting needed
   193→
   194→# Set (replace) entire arrays — each element is a separate arg
   195→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib        # Replace array
   196→tspec ts set cargo.build_std core alloc           # Replace array
   197→tspec ts set rustflags -C relocation-model=static # Replace array
   198→
   199→# Add items to arrays (append by default)
   200→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections        # Append one item
   201→tspec ts add linker.args -nostdlib -pie            # Append multiple items
   202→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles        # Insert at position 0
   203→
   204→# Remove items from arrays
   205→tspec ts remove linker.args -nostdlib              # Remove by value
   206→tspec ts remove linker.args -static -pie           # Remove multiple by value
   207→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args                   # Remove by index
   208→
   209→# Remove a field entirely
   210→tspec ts unset strip -p myapp                      # Remove scalar field
   211→tspec ts unset linker.args -t tspec-opt            # Remove array field
   212→
   213→# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)
   214→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   215→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml
   216→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup
   217→```
   218→
   219→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   220→
   221→`ts set`, `ts unset`, `ts add`, and `ts remove` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.
   222→`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.
   223→
   224→### Supported keys
   225→
   226→Keys use two forms: `FIELD` for top-level fields (`panic`, `strip`, `rustflags`) or `TABLE.FIELD` for nested fields (`cargo.profile`, `linker.args`).
   227→
   228→| Key | Type | Values |
   229→|-----|------|--------|
   230→| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |
   231→| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |
   232→| `toolchain` | scalar | e.g. `nightly`, `stable`, `nightly-2025-01-15`, `1.75` |
   233→| `rustflags` | array | e.g. `-C relocation-model=static` |
   234→| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release`, or any custom profile defined in `Cargo.toml` |
   235→| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |
   236→| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |
   237→| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |
   238→| `cargo.unstable` | array | e.g. `panic-immediate-abort` |
   239→| `cargo.config` | table | arbitrary `--config KEY=VALUE` passthrough; supports nested tables (e.g. `[cargo.config.profile.release]`) |
   240→| `cargo.build_std` | array | e.g. `core alloc` |
   241→| `linker.args` | array | e.g. `-static -nostdlib` |
   242→
   243→### Array operations
   244→
   245→Four orthogonal commands operate on arrays at two levels:
   246→
   247→| Level | Command | Purpose |
   248→|-------|---------|---------|
   249→| Field-level | `ts set` | Replace entire array with new values |
   250→| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove the array field entirely |
   251→| Item-level | `ts add` | Append items (or insert at position with `-i`) |
   252→| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items by value (or by index with `-i`) |
   253→
   254→```bash
   255→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib   # replace all with these
   256→tspec ts unset linker.args                   # clear the field
   257→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections   # append (skips duplicates)
   258→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles  # insert at position
   259→tspec ts remove linker.args -static          # remove by value
   260→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args             # remove by index
   261→```
   262→
   263→Values starting with `-` work without quoting thanks to `allow_hyphen_values`.
   264→Use `--` as an escape hatch if a value collides with a flag like `-p`, `-t`, or `-i`.
   265→
   266→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   267→
   268→## Testing
   269→
   270→```bash
   271→tspec test -p tspec           # Run tspec tests
   272→tspec test -p tspec-build     # Run tspec-build tests
   273→```
   274→
   275→## Project Structure
   276→
   277→```
   278→tspec/                  # Workspace root (also the main tspec package)
   279→  Cargo.toml            # [workspace] + [package] — workspace with root package
   280→  src/
   281→    lib.rs              # Library root, exposes modules
   282→    main.rs             # Entry point, dispatch
   283→    cli.rs              # Clap CLI definitions
   284→    types.rs            # Spec parameter types
   285→    tspec.rs            # Spec loading/saving/hashing
   286→    find_paths.rs       # Project/package/tspec/binary path discovery
   287→    workspace.rs        # Workspace package discovery
   288→    cargo_build.rs      # Build command + generated build.rs
   289→    run.rs              # Run command implementation
   290→    testing.rs          # Test command implementation
   291→    compare.rs          # Compare command (size comparison)
   292→    all.rs              # Batch operations (build_all, run_all, test_all, compare_all)
   293→    cmd/                # Command implementations (one file per command)
   294→    ts_cmd/             # Tspec management subcommands
   295→    binary.rs           # Binary operations (strip, size)
   296→  tests/
   297→    data/               # Test fixtures (TOML specs)
   298→    tspec_test.rs       # Integration tests
   299→  tspec-build/          # Library crate for build.rs integration
   300→    src/lib.rs          # Reads TSPEC_SPEC_FILE env var at build time
   301→  notes/                # Design docs and todo tracking
   302→```
   303→
   304→## Development
   305→
   306→See [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md) for current tasks.
   307→
   308→### Verification
   309→
   310→```bash
   311→tspec test -p tspec
   312→tspec test -p tspec-build
   313→tspec clippy
   314→tspec fmt --check
   315→```
   316→
   317→## Origin
   318→
   319→Originally developed as `xt` within the [rlibc-x](https://github.com/user/rlibc-x) project, then extracted to a standalone tool. The git history was preserved using `git subtree split`.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain\nfn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec) -> bool {\n    // High-level panic mode may require nightly\n    let panic_needs_nightly = spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false);\n\n    // BuildStd requires nightly\n    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();\n\n    // Unstable cargo flags require nightly\n    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();\n\n    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable\n}\n\n/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)\nfn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec) -> Result<Command> {\n    let mut cmd = Command::new(\"cargo\");\n\n    if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {\n        cmd.arg(format!(\"+{}\", tc));\n    } else if requires_nightly(spec) {\n        cmd.arg(\"+nightly\");\n    }\n\n    Ok(cmd)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Cargo operation mode\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum CargoMode {\n    Build,\n    Test,\n}\n\nimpl CargoMode {\n    fn subcommand(&self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            CargoMode::Build => \"build\",\n            CargoMode::Test => \"test\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain.\n/// For Test mode, panic=abort also needs nightly because `-Zpanic_abort_tests` is nightly-only.\nfn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> bool {\n    let panic_needs_nightly = match mode {\n        CargoMode::Test => spec\n            .panic\n            .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())\n            .unwrap_or(false),\n        CargoMode::Build => spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false),\n    };\n\n    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();\n    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();\n\n    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable\n}\n\n/// Check if spec uses an abort-like panic mode that needs `-Zpanic_abort_tests` for testing.\nfn needs_panic_abort_tests(spec: &Spec) -> bool {\n    spec.panic\n        .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())\n        .unwrap_or(false)\n}\n\n/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)\nfn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> Result<Command> {\n    let mut cmd = Command::new(\"cargo\");\n\n    if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {\n        cmd.arg(format!(\"+{}\", tc));\n    } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {\n        cmd.arg(\"+nightly\");\n    }\n\n    Ok(cmd)\n}"
}

(default). `Some(\"release\")` is equivalent to `--release`.\npub fn build_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;\n    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;\n\n    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)\n    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;\n\n    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds\n    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);\n\n    // Track if we generated a build.rs\n    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join(\"build.rs\");\n    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();\n\n    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir\n    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {\n        let s = load_spec(path)?;\n        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);\n        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;\n        (Some(s), td)\n    } else {\n        (None, None)\n    };\n\n    // Compute paths\n    let target_base = match &expanded_td {\n        Some(td) => workspace.join(\"target\").join(td),\n        None => workspace.join(\"target\"),\n    };\n    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {\n        get_binary_path(\n            &workspace,\n            &pkg_name,\n            spec,\n            cli_profile,\n            expanded_td.as_deref(),\n        )\n    } else {\n        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, cli_profile)\n    };\n\n    // Validate the effective profile exists before invoking cargo\n    let effective_profile = spec\n        .as_ref()\n        .and_then(|s| s.cargo.profile.as_deref())\n        .or(cli_profile);\n    if let Some(profile) = effective_profile {\n        validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;\n    }\n\n    // Check for misconfigurations before running cargo\n    let spec_warnings = if let Some(spec) = &spec {\n        check_spec_misconfigurations(&pkg_name, spec, &pkg_dir)\n    } else {\n        Vec::new()\n    };\n\n    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo build\n    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {\n        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();\n        println!(\"Building {} with spec {}\", pkg_name, path.display());\n\n        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed\n        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();\n        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join(\"src/main.rs\").exists();\n        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {\n            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;\n        }\n\n        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec)?;\n        cmd.arg(\"build\");\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n\n        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs\n        cmd.env(\"TSPEC_SPEC_FILE\", path.as_os_str());\n\n        apply_spec_to_command(\n            &mut cmd,\n            spec,\n            &workspace,\n            cli_profile,\n            expanded_td.as_deref(),\n        )?;\n        cmd.status().context(\"failed to run cargo\")?\n    } else {\n        println!(\"Building {} (no tspec)\", pkg_name);\n        let mut cmd = Command::new(\"cargo\");\n        cmd.arg(\"build\");\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {\n            match p {\n                \"debug\" | \"dev\" => {}\n                _ => {\n                    cmd.arg(\"--profile\").arg(p);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        cmd.status().context(\"failed to run cargo\")?\n    };\n\n    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)\n    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {\n        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);\n    }\n\n    if !status.success() {\n        match &tspec_path {\n            Some(path) => {\n                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();\n                bail!(\n                    \"cargo build failed for `{}` with spec {}\",\n                    pkg_name,\n                    display_path\n                )\n            }\n            None => bail!(\"cargo build failed for `{}`\", pkg_name),\n        }\n    }\n\n    println!(\"  {}\", binary_path.display());\n    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);\n    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);\n    Ok(BuildResult {\n        binary_path,\n        target_base,\n    })\n}\n\n/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.\n/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.\npub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some(\"release\"))\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations.\n///\n/// Handles spec loading, build.rs generation, command construction, and cleanup.\n/// `cli_profile` is the profile from the CLI (e.g., \"release\", \"release-small\").\n/// `None` means debug (default). `Some(\"release\")` is equivalent to `--release`.\npub fn run_cargo(\n    mode: CargoMode,\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;\n    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;\n\n    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)\n    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;\n\n    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds\n    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);\n\n    // Track if we generated a build.rs\n    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join(\"build.rs\");\n    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();\n\n    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir\n    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {\n        let s = load_spec(path)?;\n        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);\n        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;\n        (Some(s), td)\n    } else {\n        (None, None)\n    };\n\n    // Compute paths\n    let target_base = match &expanded_td {\n        Some(td) => workspace.join(\"target\").join(td),\n        None => workspace.join(\"target\"),\n    };\n    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {\n        get_binary_path(\n            &workspace,\n            &pkg_name,\n            spec,\n            cli_profile,\n            expanded_td.as_deref(),\n        )\n    } else {\n        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, cli_profile)\n    };\n\n    // Validate the effective profile exists before invoking cargo\n    let effective_profile = spec\n        .as_ref()\n        .and_then(|s| s.cargo.profile.as_deref())\n        .or(cli_profile);\n    if let Some(profile) = effective_profile {\n        validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;\n    }\n\n    // Check for misconfigurations before running cargo\n    let spec_warnings = if let Some(spec) = &spec {\n        check_spec_misconfigurations(&pkg_name, spec, &pkg_dir)\n    } else {\n        Vec::new()\n    };\n\n    let verb = match mode {\n        CargoMode::Build => \"Building\",\n        CargoMode::Test => \"Testing\",\n    };\n\n    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo subcommand\n    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {\n        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();\n        println!(\"{} {} with spec {}\", verb, pkg_name, path.display());\n\n        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed\n        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();\n        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join(\"src/main.rs\").exists();\n        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {\n            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;\n        }\n\n        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec, mode)?;\n        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n\n        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs\n        cmd.env(\"TSPEC_SPEC_FILE\", path.as_os_str());\n\n        apply_spec_to_command(\n            &mut cmd,\n            spec,\n            &workspace,\n            cli_profile,\n            expanded_td.as_deref(),\n        )?;\n\n        // For test mode, append -Zpanic_abort_tests to RUSTFLAGS if needed\n        if mode == CargoMode::Test && needs_panic_abort_tests(spec) {\n            let existing = cmd\n                .get_envs()\n                .find(|(k, _)| k == &\"RUSTFLAGS\")\n                .and_then(|(_, v)| v)\n                .map(|v| v.to_string_lossy().into_owned())\n                .unwrap_or_default();\n            let new_flags = if existing.is_empty() {\n                \"-Zpanic_abort_tests\".to_string()\n            } else {\n                format!(\"{} -Zpanic_abort_tests\", existing)\n            };\n            cmd.env(\"RUSTFLAGS\", new_flags);\n        }\n\n        cmd.status()\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"failed to run cargo {}\", mode.subcommand()))?\n    } else {\n        println!(\"{} {} (no tspec)\", verb, pkg_name);\n        let mut cmd = Command::new(\"cargo\");\n        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {\n            match p {\n                \"debug\" | \"dev\" => {}\n                _ => {\n                    cmd.arg(\"--profile\").arg(p);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        cmd.status()\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"failed to run cargo {}\", mode.subcommand()))?\n    };\n\n    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)\n    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {\n        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);\n    }\n\n    if !status.success() {\n        match &tspec_path {\n            Some(path) => {\n                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();\n                bail!(\n                    \"cargo {} failed for `{}` with spec {}\",\n                    mode.subcommand(),\n                    pkg_name,\n                    display_path\n                )\n            }\n            None => bail!(\"cargo {} failed for `{}`\", mode.subcommand(), pkg_name),\n        }\n    }\n\n    if mode == CargoMode::Build {\n        println!(\"  {}\", binary_path.display());\n    }\n    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);\n    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);\n    Ok(BuildResult {\n        binary_path,\n        target_base,\n    })\n}\n\n/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.\npub fn build_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile)\n}\n\n/// Test a package with a spec.\npub fn test_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n) -> Result<()> {\n    run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile)?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.\n/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.\npub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some(\"release\"))\n}"
}

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
